⏺ T14 is on its own branch, milestone-e-monitoring, created from main. I read
  the code it touches and below is the plan. I haven't changed any code yet. I 
  need your approval first.

  T14 plan: metrics and live status SSE

  Part A — Metrics (D26). No new dependency; client_golang is already installed.
  - HTTP middleware in internal/obs: wraps the api, worker and mock-provider
    handlers.
    - Records scc_http_requests_total{route,code} and a duration histogram.
    - The route label is the matched pattern (r.Pattern, e.g. GET 
      /api/campaigns/{id}), never the raw path, so the number of label values
      stays small.
  - Worker (dispatch.go):
    - scc_send_attempts_total{channel,outcome} counts every outcome. HTTP 429s
      are the outcome="rate_limited" series, so there is no separate 429 metric.
    - scc_send_retries_total{channel} counts messages put back in the queue for
      a retry.
    - scc_provider_request_duration_seconds{channel} is a latency histogram.
  - API (in Interpret and generateContent):
    scc_llm_calls_total{operation,result}.
    - operation is interpret or content.
    - result is ok, error or invalid. invalid means the answer failed
      validation, which is what triggers the retry.
  - Tests: counters change after a fake provider call, an LLM call, and an HTTP
    request. The route label is the pattern.

  Part B — GET /api/campaigns/{id}/events (SSE, behind auth).
  - Polling: the handler reads one database snapshot every ~1s. It sends event: 
    status only when the JSON differs from the last one it sent. The interval
    can be changed for tests.
  - Snapshot contents:
    - The campaign's status, send_at, audience_built_at, completed_at and
      approval_estimate.
    - Per channel:
      - The actual audience, meaning messages created.
      - Counts for each of the 7 statuses.
      - retries (attempts after the first) and rate_limited (429s).
      - Skip reasons and their counts.
      - Failure reasons: failed messages grouped by last_error, top 10, so the
        payload stays small.
      - uncertain, the number of messages that may have been delivered without
        confirmation.
      - resendable: the same audience as BuildResend, so the number matches what
        a resend would actually send.
      - active_resend_id.
  - Errors:
    - Unknown id → 404 before the stream opens.
    - A database error mid-stream → event: error and the stream closes.
  - Tests:
    - The counts query on a fixture: every status, reasons, retries and 429s,
      resendable count, and estimate vs actual.
    - Unchanged counts → one event. A change → another event.
    - Client disconnect → the handler returns.
    - Unknown id → 404.

  Docs: README gets a metrics list and the events endpoint. There is no new
  decision for DECISIONS.md unless you choose otherwise below.

  Steps: A, then B, using TDD. Then the full make test, build and lint, then
  commits.

  One decision for you: what should the stream do once a campaign is completed
  or cancelled?
  - (a) Recommended: keep the stream open until the client leaves. It's the
    simplest option: a finished campaign's counts don't change, so no further
    events are sent. The cost is a cheap 1s query per open tab.
  - (b) Send the final snapshot, then event: end, and close. This saves those
    queries, but the frontend must close its EventSource when it gets end.
    Otherwise the browser reconnects every few seconds.

  Do you approve the plan, and which option do you want, (a) or (b)?

  I haven't run any tests yet. ai-sessions/05-campaign-development.txt is still
  uncommitted; please check it for keys and personal data before committing it.

⏺ T14 is finished on milestone-e-monitoring, in 3 commits. Nothing is pushed or
  merged.

  Metrics (D26). Each process's /metrics now has:
  - HTTP requests: counts and durations, labelled by route pattern (e.g. GET 
    /api/campaigns/{id}), so campaign ids don't create new series.
  - Worker: provider attempts by outcome (429s are the rate_limited outcome),
    retries, and provider latency.
  - API: AI calls by ok / invalid / error / cancelled.

  To get the real route on guarded /api/ paths, I added one line to
  auth.Require.

  GET /api/campaigns/{id}/events (D36).
  - It sends a status snapshot straight away, then again only when something
    changes (checked every second).
  - The snapshot holds every count from the plan, plus "uncertain deliveries"
    and how many a resend would reach now. That number is computed exactly the
    way a resend is built, so it matches.
  - Once the campaign is finished, it sends the final snapshot, then end, and
    closes. An unknown id gets a 404.

  One change to what you approved: the stream ends a bit later than "completed 
  or cancelled". After a cancel, messages a worker is already sending still get
  their final status, up to the 30s lease. If the stream closed right at the
  cancel, its last snapshot would be wrong. So it ends only once no message is
  still in progress. I wrote this into D36. Say if you want it handled
  differently.

  Checks:
  - Tests: make test passes, and so do make lint and the image build. The new
    SSE tests passed on their first run, so I broke the code on purpose twice;
    the tests caught both, and the code was restored.
  - Live run: I recreated your running app containers with the new image
    (database volume untouched) and sent a 252-user SMS campaign. The stream
    ended with 252 sent, matching the estimate. Retries (32) equal the worker's
    429 and 5xx count, and the provider's own metrics agree.

  Open risks:
  - Each open stream runs about five small queries per second, more when a
    resend count is shown. That's fine at this data size, but nothing limits the
    number of open streams.
  - If no worker is running, a cancelled campaign's stream stays open until its
    in-flight messages are reclaimed.

  Searched for 1 pattern, ran 4 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ "1 duplicate?" comes from the ponytail plugin. Ponytail draws the status band
  you're looking at (hooks/band/register.tsx), including the "50% reuse" and
  "tests" items. The label means: one function I wrote this session has the same
  name as a function that already existed in another file. Case and _ are
  ignored when names are compared. The question mark is there because the check
  compares names only and can't tell whether the code does the same thing.

  What it flagged: my new test helper obstest.Value
  (internal/obstest/obstest.go:14) has the same name as existing value()
  methods. Those are in internal/audience/sql.go:132 (and others in that file)
  and internal/content/template.go:39.

  It's a false alarm. Those methods return an audience rule's value or a
  template variable's value. Mine reads a metric's value from the Prometheus
  registry in tests. They only share the word "value". The plugin's own code
  calls this out as a known limit: shortcut: names only. So there's nothing to
  reuse or merge.

  The band only watches files changed with the Write/Edit tools. I made most of
  this session's code changes with shell scripts, so the "reuse" percentage only
  reflects part of the work.

  ## T14 review: metrics and the live status stream (c81179f..35be409)

  I ran the tests for the packages this branch touches (campaign, obs, auth,
  worker) in the Docker test profile, and all passed. I did not run the full
  suite, the build or the linter. I reviewed in one pass, read the code around
  the diff (queue.go Claim/Complete/FinishCampaigns, approve.go cancel/resend,
  the migrations, auth.Routes and main.go serve) and changed no files.

  ### Strengths
  - **Labels stay bounded.** The route label is the matched mux pattern, and
  anything unmatched becomes `"unmatched"`, so ids never create new series.
  Copying `r.Pattern` back in `auth.Require` (auth.go:69-71) is a small, correct
  fix, and `TestRequire_GuardedRouteLabelsMetrics` pins it.
  - **SSE still works behind the middleware.** `statusWriter` passes Flush
  through and has `Unwrap`, so SSE and `http.ResponseController` keep working.
  The obs test checks Flush survives.
  - **The end-of-stream rule is correct.** I traced it against the worker code.
  Cancel leaves `sending` messages alone; a reclaimed lease stays `sending`;
  Complete turns `retry_scheduled` into `cancelled` for a cancelled campaign;
  and the audience builder takes a lock that cancel waits on. So once the status
  is completed or cancelled and nothing is in flight, nothing can change, and
  `end` is never sent too early. `TestEvents_EndsAfterFinalSnapshot` covers the
  cancel-while-sending case.
  - **"Only on change" is deterministic.** It compares the marshalled JSON. Go
  sorts map keys and the failure-reason query has a total order (`ORDER BY 3
  DESC, 2`), so there are no false re-sends.
  - **Plan coverage is complete.** Every T14 item is there: counts by status,
  retries, 429s, skip and failure reasons, estimate vs actual, resendable count,
  change-only sending, disconnect. T18 also gets `send_at` vs
  `audience_built_at` (scheduled vs actual start) and `active_resend_id` to
  decide whether to offer "Resend to N".
  - **Tests use a real server and real DB, with low flake risk.** The poll
  interval is 20 ms against 100-200 ms quiet windows and a 3 s timeout. No test
  uses `t.Parallel`, so the before/after metric deltas from `obstest` are safe.
  - **Metrics are recorded once per real attempt.** That includes `interrupted`,
  and the retry counter is correctly not incremented for interrupted attempts.
  Extracting `startSSE` is a clean, behaviour-preserving refactor.

  ### Issues

  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **One open dashboard makes every API shutdown slow and fail**
  (cmd/scc/main.go:137-157 together with events.go:79-100).
     - **What's wrong:** `http.Server.Shutdown` does not cancel the contexts of
  requests already running; it waits for handlers to return. The events handler
  only returns on client disconnect or campaign end. For a draft, approved,
  scheduled or sending campaign it polls forever.
     - **Failure scenario:** a marketer has a sending campaign's dashboard open
  and the api gets SIGTERM (`docker compose stop`, redeploy). Shutdown blocks
  for the full 10 s, returns `context.DeadlineExceeded`, and `serve` returns it.
  The process logs `"exiting" err=context deadline exceeded` and exits with
  code 1. Docker's default stop grace period is also 10 s, so the container is
  likely SIGKILLed before that. Every normal restart with a dashboard open now
  looks like a crash.
     - **Why this is new:** content SSE had the same pattern before T14, but it
  was bounded by the AI timeout. Status streams have no bound.
     - **Fix:** give streams a "server is stopping" signal. One option: a
  context the handler selects on, cancelled from `srv.RegisterOnShutdown`. The
  stream then returns and the browser's EventSource reconnects to the next
  instance. Avoid `BaseContext` cancellation for all requests, because that
  would also abort in-flight content generation. Add a test that Shutdown
  returns promptly with an open stream.

  2. **The stream uses an event named `error`, which clashes with EventSource's
  own `error` event** (events.go:92).
     - **What's wrong:** T18 consumes this stream with EventSource (plan line
  224). EventSource fires its own `error` event on connection failures, so
  `addEventListener('error', …)` receives both the server's MessageEvent (with
  `data`) and plain connection-error Events (no `data`).
     - **Failure scenario:** a dashboard handler does
  `JSON.parse(e.data).message` and throws on every reconnect blip. Or it treats
  every reconnect as a server error and closes the stream for good.
     - **Why this is new:** the content stream uses a fetch-based reader, so the
  name was harmless there.
     - **Fix:** rename this stream's event (e.g. `failure`) and document it in
  the README row, or send nothing and just close so EventSource reconnects.
  Cheapest to change now, before T18 is written against it.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  3. **The snapshot is not read at one point in time** (events.go:103-189). It
  runs four or more separate statements outside a transaction, so one snapshot
  can mix moments; for example, retries can include an attempt whose message
  status was read as still `sending`. The next tick fixes it, and the final
  snapshot is consistent because nothing changes after the end condition.
  Wrapping the queries in a `REPEATABLE READ` read-only transaction would fix
  this; it is one `BeginTx` call.
  4. **DB load grows with open tabs** (events.go:73-100). Each tab costs about
  4-6 queries per second, plus a BuildResend count over `users` per channel once
  character-limit skips exist. These run on the shared pgxpool, whose default
  size is max(4, CPUs). With a few dozen tabs, that competes with campaign and
  auth requests for connections. It is fine for this demo scope (indexes
  `messages_campaign_status_idx` and `delivery_attempts UNIQUE(message_id,
  attempt_no)` cover the lookups). Worth one line under known limitations in the
  README; a shared per-campaign poller is the upgrade path.
  5. **A message channel missing from `campaigns.channels` would panic the
  handler** (events.go:128 and 151). `s.Channels[ch]` would be nil. Current code
  cannot produce such a row, and net/http recovers the panic, but a nil check
  (`if cs == nil { continue }`) costs one line.
  6. **The interpret metric counts a client disconnect as `error`**
  (interpret.go:75-77). Content records `cancelled` for the same thing, so the
  README's definition of `cancelled` ("the client left") holds for content only.
  7. **Two different "retries" numbers** (dispatch.go:268 vs events.go:144).
  `scc_send_retries_total` counts retries scheduled; the snapshot's `retries`
  counts attempts after a message's first, interrupted attempts included. Both
  are defensible, but say which one the dashboard shows so the two are not
  compared.
  8. **SSE durations distort the request-duration histogram.** The histogram
  uses default buckets (up to 10 s), so long streams all land in +Inf for the
  events and content routes. The Help text says SSE is included. Acceptable as
  is; excluding `text/event-stream` responses would be cleaner.
  9. **D36's "Why" overstates it** (DECISIONS.md:287). A finished parent's
  `active_resend_id` (resend created or cancelled) and its `resendable` count
  can still change after `end`. T18 must re-fetch after the marketer clicks
  Resend, rather than expect the stream to show it.

  ### Declined to judge
  - **Per-campaign authorisation on the stream:** any logged-in marketer can
  stream any campaign. This matches every other campaign endpoint (campaigns are
  shared between marketers), so it is not a T14 regression.
  - **No metric for skipped messages** (consent or character limit): D26 lists
  attempts, 429s, retries, latency, Claude calls and HTTP only; skips are on the
  dashboard via the DB.
  - **The `client_model` dependency moving from indirect to direct in go.mod:**
  same module and version, now imported by the `obstest` helper. Not a new
  dependency in the CLAUDE.md sense.
  - **Streams for draft campaigns stay open forever:** the plan and D36 only
  define an end for finished campaigns. The cost is covered by issues 1 and 4.
  - **Frontend reconnect behaviour after `end`:** belongs to T18. D36 already
  records the trade-off.

  ### Recommendations
  - Fix issues 1 and 2 before merging. Each is a few lines and saves later
  rework (operations for #1, T18 for #2).
  - Optionally fold in issues 3, 5 and 6, which are each one or two lines, and
  add a known-limitations note for issue 4 in the README.
  - Before calling T14 done, run the full `make test`, the build and lint, as
  CLAUDE.md requires.

  ### Assessment
  **Ready to merge?** With fixes.

  **Reasoning:** T14 matches the plan, its correctness is sound, and its tests
  are real. However, never-ending status streams now turn every graceful API
  shutdown into a 10 s timeout with exit code 1. And the `error` event name will
  trip up the EventSource-based dashboard planned in T18.

  I did not check the frontend side (it does not exist yet), BuildResend's query
  plan on large data, or behaviour beyond the 3,000-user seed. The main
  operational risk to tell the user about is issue 1, which affects every
  redeploy.

⏺ The review is back: no critical issues, 2 important and 7 minor. I checked
  each against the code:

  - #1 Shutdown blocked by open streams: valid. http.Server.Shutdown waits for
    running handlers and doesn't cancel their contexts, and the status stream
    never returns for a campaign that isn't finished. I'll fix it with TDD: the
    handler also stops when the process is shutting down, and the browser
    reconnects.
  - #2 error event clashes with EventSource: valid. I'll drop the event and just
    close. EventSource then reconnects, and the reconnect's first snapshot
    either works or returns a 500, which stops reconnection for good.
  - Minor fixes I'll make: a consistent read for each snapshot (#3), README
    notes on the database load and on what "retries" means in each place (#4,
    #7), the cancelled result for interpretation too (#6), and a clearer D36
    trade-off (#9).
  - Skipping:
    - #5: no code path can create such a message row.
    - #8: the help text already says the duration histogram includes SSE
      streams.

      286  - **Decision:** `GET /api/campaigns/{id}/events` sends a statu
           s snapshot when the counts change (polled ~1s, D16). Once the 
           campaign is `completed` or `cancelled` and no message is still
            in flight (after a cancel, messages a worker holds finish lat
           er), it sends the final snapshot, then an `end` event, and clo
           ses.
      287  - **Why:** A finished campaign never changes, so no database p
           olling for open dashboards of old campaigns.
      288  - **Rejected:** *Keep the stream open until the client leaves:
           * one query per second per open tab for nothing.
      289 -- **Trade-offs:** The frontend must close its `EventSource` on
          - `end`, or the browser reconnects every few seconds.          
      289 +- **Trade-offs:** The frontend must close its `EventSource` on
          + `end`, or the browser reconnects every few seconds. A resend 
          +created after `end` changes the parent's resend fields without
          + a new event, so the frontend fetches the campaign again after
          + a resend.

⏺ The README has no known-limitations section yet, so I'll check where one fits:

⏺ PR #5 is open with the review fixes pushed, and it isn't merged:
  https://github.com/[github-user]/smart-campaign-composer/pull/5

  Review result: no critical issues, 2 important and 7 minor. The reviewer's
  verdict was "ready to merge with fixes". I checked every finding against the
  code before acting on it.

  Fixed (important):
  1. An open dashboard broke every API shutdown. A status stream never ends on
     its own, so stopping the API waited the full 10 s and exited as a failure.
     Streams now close when the server stops, and the browser reconnects. I
     removed the fix on purpose and the test caught it. On the real stack,
     docker compose stop api with a stream open now takes 1 s and exits cleanly.
  2. The stream's error event would have clashed with the browser's own error 
     event. The browser's EventSource, which the T18 dashboard will use, fires
     an error event of its own on connection problems. On a database error the
     stream now just closes and the browser reconnects.

  Fixed (minor):
  - Each snapshot is now read from the database at a single point in time.
  - An interpretation the client walked away from now counts as cancelled, the
    same as content generation.
  - The README now explains the two different "retries" numbers and says that a
    resend created after end isn't streamed (also added to D36's trade-offs). It
    also has a new Known limitations section covering the polling load.

  Not changed, with reasons in the PR:
  - A nil check for a message on a channel the campaign doesn't use: no code
    path can create such a row.
  - Long streams in the request-duration histogram: the metric's description
    already says streams are included.

  After the fixes, make test, make lint and the image build all pass. The PR
  description now includes the review results.

  T18 needs to close its EventSource on end and fetch the campaign again after a
  resend. ai-sessions/05-campaign-development.txt is still uncommitted; please
  export this session and check both files for keys and personal data before
  committing.

⏺ PR #5 is merged into main and milestone-f-frontend is created from it. I
  haven't changed any code; the audit only read the code. It compares the
  current campaign API with what tasks T15–T18 and the decisions need.

  What already works for the frontend

  - List: GET /api/campaigns returns the newest 200 with status, send_at,
    version, parent_campaign_id, approval_estimate and timestamps. That's enough
    for the "Scheduled for …" state and resend links.
  - Draft editing: rules, proposed schedule and content each have a PUT with
    version checks. Each returns the full campaign with refreshed counts, and
    the error shapes are consistent (error, plus field_errors for rules and
    content).
  - Lifecycle: approve, cancel, resend and estimate all work, plus the live
    stream from T14.

  Gaps

  Blocks T16 (needs a decision):
  1. There's no catalog endpoint. The chip editor (D9) needs the list of fields,
     allowed operators and value shapes, plus the real cities and categories.
     Today these exist only inside the server (fieldOps and LoadCatalog), and no
     API returns them. Without one, the frontend would have to copy the field
     list by hand, and the copy would drift.

  Product gaps (each needs a decision):

  2. Channels can't be changed after a draft is created. There's no endpoint for
     it. If the marketer changes the channel picker, they must create a new
     draft, which runs the AI again.
  3. The brief can't be edited or re-interpreted on an existing draft.
     Rephrasing also means a new draft.
  4. "Reopening" a campaign isn't possible, and D11 rules it out on purpose.
     There's no way back from approved or scheduled to draft, and a cancelled
     campaign stays cancelled. Fixing a scheduled campaign means cancelling it
     and starting over. A cheap middle ground would be "duplicate as a new
     draft", but that's new scope.
  5. Drafts can't be deleted or discarded. Abandoned drafts pile up in the list,
     and the list has no paging or filter beyond the newest 200.

  Not gaps, but the frontend must handle them:
  - Every chip edit saves and bumps the version. So rule edits while content is
    streaming make the content save fail with "the campaign changed", and the UI
    should lock editing during generation.
  - A proposed send_at can go stale while the draft sits. The server rejects it
    at approval, so the UI should show it as a suggestion only.
  - Responses include only created_by and approved_by as ids. Showing names
    would need a small addition.

  First decision: the catalog endpoint (gap 1)

  I recommend GET /api/catalog, returning the fields with their operators and
  value shapes, plus the cities and categories from the database. It's one small
  handler built on LoadCatalog and the existing field list, with a test, and
  the server stays the only source of the rule language. The alternative is to
  hard-code the field list in the frontend and add only cities and categories to
  an endpoint. That's less backend work, but the two copies of the rule
  language can drift apart.

  Should I add GET /api/catalog as the first step of Milestone F? After that
  I'll ask about gaps 2–5 one at a time.

❯ i'm already on milestone-f-frontend. let's keep the backend changes needed

⏺ I reviewed the code and schema for all three: cancel works today, and there is
  no delete and no archive. I haven't implemented anything.

  Current state

  - Cancel (D11) works only on approved or sending campaigns. It records who
    cancelled and when, cancels the messages still waiting, and lets messages
    already being sent finish. This works as designed and I'd keep it as is.
  - Delete: no endpoint. The foreign keys have no cascade (content, messages and
    attempts point at the campaign, and a resend points at its parent).
  - Archive: nothing exists.

  A real bug: an abandoned resend draft blocks resends for good

  A resend starts as a draft, and the "one active resend per campaign and
  channel" index counts every resend that isn't cancelled. A draft can't be
  cancelled, so if a marketer opens "Resend" and walks away, that channel can
  never be resent again. Every later attempt returns 409 "already has an active
  resend", and nothing in the API can clear it. Whatever we decide below must
  fix this.

  Options for drafts the marketer gives up on

  - A. Hard delete, drafts only (recommended): DELETE /api/campaigns/{id} with
    the version.
    - Deletes: the draft and its content, in one transaction, only if it's still
      a draft at that version. A draft has no messages, so no delivery history
      is lost.
    - Fixes the bug: deleting a resend draft frees its slot.
    - Concurrent edits: if another marketer approves it at the same moment, one
      of the two requests gets 409. A content stream still running gets
      "campaign changed" when it tries to save.
    - Never deletable: anything approved or later. Messages and attempts are the
      audit trail and the basis for duplicate prevention (D4, D31).
  - B. Allow cancelling drafts: the draft becomes cancelled with who and when.
    This is the smallest code change, and it also fixes the bug. But "cancelled"
    then mixes "stopped a send" with "never sent", the dashboard has to tell
    them apart, and abandoned drafts still clutter the list.

  Archiving

  I recommend not building it. Its only use is hiding old campaigns from the
  list. Hard-deleting drafts removes most of the clutter, and the list can be
  filtered by status in the frontend. Archiving would need a column, a list
  filter and an unarchive action, and nothing in CASE.md asks for it. It can be
  added later without changing anything above.

  First decision: for drafts, do you want A (hard delete, drafts only, 
  version-checked) or B (cancel drafts)? Once that's settled I'll confirm the
  archiving choice, then record both in DECISIONS.md before writing any code.

  committed on milestone-f-frontend, not pushed. Cancel and the campaign

  What changed
  - Delete: DELETE /api/campaigns/{id} with {"version"} removes a draft and its
    content in one transaction and returns 204. Approved, sending, completed and
    cancelled campaigns get 409 and are never deleted. A stale version also
    gets 409, a missing version 422, and an unknown id 404.
  - Concurrent requests: the delete locks the draft first. A delete racing an
    approve, or a content save, ends with exactly one of them succeeding.
  - Decisions: D37 records the deletion behaviour, and archiving is in the
    Deferred list.
  - README: a row for the new endpoint, a note on the cancel row that drafts are
    deleted instead, and a new Future improvements section with archiving.

  Tests: they were written first and failed (405, no route) before the code
  existed. They cover:
  - deleting a draft with its content
  - every rejection case, with nothing removed by a rejected delete
  - the resend bug: a second resend is 409 while the abandoned draft exists, and
    201 after deleting it
  - delete racing approve: run 30 times, all passed, though I didn't confirm
    both orderings actually came up
  - delete during content generation: the stream ends with error and nothing is
    written back

  Checks
  - make lint passes.
  - make test: one end-to-end test failed,
    TestLifecycle_ShutdownWhileWaiting_ReleasesTheMessage. It passed 10 of 10
    runs on its own, and its package passed on a full re-run.
    - The machine was heavily loaded (load average 50–118).
    - The test gives the worker a 50 ms shutdown deadline that also covers a
      database read. I think a slow read uses up that deadline, which makes it a
      timing flake that predates this change. I didn't capture the failure
      output, so that cause isn't confirmed.
    - I can make the deadline more robust separately if you want.

  Next: gap 2 from the audit, changing channels on an existing draft, then the
  catalog endpoint, which T16 needs. Which do you want first?

⏺ I've confirmed the bug: the message stays sending because the deadline expires
  during the initial database read, before reaching the rate limiter—and I also
  found the test never truly exercised "shutdown while waiting" since a
  deadline causes Wait to fail instantly. I'll fix this by canceling the context
  only once Tokens() drops below 0, a deterministic signal that the worker is
  actually blocked in the limiter.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ The flaky shutdown test is fixed and committed (6ed21a6). The three features
  each change business behaviour, so I'm proposing how each should work before
  writing any code.

  Flaky shutdown test: fixed

  - Cause (reproduced): the test shut the worker down after a fixed 50 ms. Under
    load, that ran out during the worker's first database read, so the message
    was left sending. Also, a deadline makes the rate limiter fail at once
    instead of waiting, so the test never really covered "shutdown while
    waiting".
  - Fix: the test now shuts down exactly when the worker is blocked in the
    limiter (its reserved token makes the count drop below 0), with no timing
    guess.
  - Same problem elsewhere: another shutdown test and three related tests waited
    fixed times for "the provider accepted the hung request" or "some messages
    were sent". Under load one of them failed: a retry hit a key still in
    progress and the message was marked failed. They now wait for the actual
    condition.
  - Result: 100 of 100 runs passed with the machine heavily loaded. Removing the
    release-on-shutdown code on purpose still makes the test fail.

  Proposed behaviour, for your approval

  These rules apply to all three:
  - Drafts only: any other status gets 409, so approved campaigns are untouched.
  - Version checks: each call needs the version, and a stale one gets 409. Each
    successful change bumps the version and returns the campaign with refreshed
    counts.
  - Resend drafts are fixed: changing their channel or brief gets 409, the same
    as their rules today.

  1. PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/channels {channels, version}
  - Validation: the same as on create (sms and/or email, no duplicates).
  - A removed channel's content is deleted in the same transaction. It has to
    be: approval rejects content for a channel that isn't chosen
    (ValidateDraft), so leftover content would block approval.
  - An added channel starts with no content. Approval stays blocked until it's
    written or generated, which is the existing rule.

  2. GET /api/catalog (read-only, needs login)
  - Fields: each field with its operators and value type (city list, category
    list, days or a fixed date, count, amount), taken from the same table the
    server validates against.
  - Values and limits: the current cities and categories from the database, plus
    the limits (20 rules, 365 days, and so on), so the chip editor can't drift
    from the server.

  3. PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/request {request, version}: edit and re-interpret 
  the brief
  - Version check twice: once before the AI call, so no AI credits are spent on
    a stale draft, and again when saving.
  - On success, the brief text, the rules and the proposed send time (with its
    note) are all replaced by the new interpretation. Manual chip edits are
    overwritten, which is the point of re-interpreting. The frontend will ask
    for confirmation first.
  - On failure (unsupported brief, AI invalid twice, timeout, or AI
    unavailable): the same 422/502/504 errors as create, and the draft is
    unchanged.
  - Content is kept as it is. The marketer reviews it before approval anyway and
    can regenerate it. Clearing it would silently throw away hand edits. The
    response won't flag the content as stale; T17 can show a hint.

  Do you approve all three as proposed? The one I most want your view on is in
  item 3: keep the content after re-interpreting (recommended), or clear it?
  Once you approve, I'll record the decisions and build them in your order
  (channels, catalog, brief), with TDD and nothing pushed.

          ted and cancelled campaigns are never deleted (409); stop a sen
          d with cancel (D37). |
      49  | `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/rules` `{"rules", "version"}` | Repl
          ace the draft's rules; they are validated and recounted. |
      50 +| `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/channels` `{"channels", "version"}` 
         +| Change a draft's channels, validated as on create; counts are
         + refreshed. A removed channel's content is deleted; an added ch
         +annel has no content until it is written or generated, and appr
         +oval waits for it (D38). A resend's channel is fixed (409). |  
      51  | `PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/schedule` `{"send_at", "version"}` |
           Change or clear (`null`) the proposed send time: RFC 3339 with
           offset, between now and 30 days ahead. |
      52  | `GET /api/campaigns`, `GET /api/campaigns/{id}` | List (newes
          t 200) / one campaign; drafts include current counts, a single 
          campaign its content. |
      53  | `POST /api/campaigns/{id}/content` `{"version"}` | The AI wri
          tes the messages for the chosen channels, in the brief's langua
          ge. The response is a `text/event-stream`: `delta` `{field, tex
          t}` events while it writes (fields `sms.body`, `email.subject`,
           `email.body`), `retry` `{errors}` if the first answer was inva
          lid, then `done` `{content, version}` or `error` `{message}`. C
          ontent is saved only when valid and only if the client is still
           connected. |

⏺ All three features are built in the order you asked, and so is the
  shutdown-test fix. Everything is committed on milestone-f-frontend and not
  pushed. The full make test did not fully pass: two tests failed in untouched
  packages (details below).

  Recorded: D38–D40 in DECISIONS.md. The frontend requirements are in the plan:
  confirm before removing a channel that has content (T16), confirm before
  re-interpreting overwrites hand-edited rules (T16), and a clear warning after
  re-interpretation that the content should be reviewed or regenerated (T17).

  Built (each with a README row, tests written first):
  1. PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/channels: drafts only, with a version check. A
     removed channel's content is deleted in the same transaction, and an added
     channel has no content, so approval waits for it. Resend drafts, approved
     campaigns and stale versions get 409, and a rejected request changes
     nothing.
  2. GET /api/catalog: fields with operators and value types, the cities and
     categories from the database, and the limits. Requires login.
     - The value types sit next to the validator's own field list. A test runs a
       sample of every type through the real validator, so the catalog can't
       drift from what the server accepts.
     - It only checks shape, though: labelling an amount field as count wouldn't
       be caught.
  3. PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/request:
     - On success: the brief, rules and proposed send time are replaced; content
       is kept.
     - On failure (unsupported, AI invalid, or empty brief): the draft is
       unchanged.
     - Version checks: the version is checked before the AI call, and stale,
       approved, resend and unknown requests make no AI call at all. A change
       made while the AI is reading gets 409 instead of being overwritten.
     - I removed each version check on purpose, and its test failed each time.

  Checks:
  - Focused tests for every feature pass, and so does make lint.
  - make test: everything passed except two tests in packages I didn't touch,
    TestSend_ConcurrentSameKey (provider) and
    TestProcess_CancelDuringRateLimitWait (worker). Both passed 10 of 10 when
    rerun on their own, and the machine was heavily loaded (load average
    about 65).
  - I didn't capture their failure messages, but each relies on a fixed delay of
    the same kind as the shutdown test:
    - The provider test sleeps 50 ms and assumes the first request's key is
      stored by then.
    - The worker test cancels after 100 ms and assumes that lands before the
      rate limiter's next token at 400 ms.

  Should I fix those two the same way, waiting on the actual condition instead 
  of a delay? For the provider test that means waiting until the first key is
  stored. For the worker test it means waiting until the worker is blocked in
  the limiter. The worker test still needs a time margin: the limiter can't be
  released early, so the cancel must commit before the next token. I'd make it
  fail with a clear message, never pass silently, when that doesn't happen. With
  those two fixed, the suite would be fully green before you review the branch.

✻ Brewed for 36s · done Thursday 9:33 PM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Review: 952e7b7..21e8d05 (D37–D40 backend, test-sync fixes)

  I reviewed the full diff and the code it touches: http.go, content.go
  (generateContent, saveContent, updateContent), approve.go (approve, cancel,
  resend, estimate), interpret.go, events.go, rules.go, sql.go (LoadCatalog),
  dispatch.go around the limiter wait, and the migrations (FKs,
  campaigns_one_active_resend_idx).

  I ran the tests in Docker and all passed: `./internal/campaign` (23.9s),
  `./internal/audience`, `./internal/e2e` (60.4s), `./internal/provider`,
  `./internal/worker` (30.1s). I did not run them with `-race`, and I did not
  run lint or the full build.

  ### Strengths
  - **Locking is correct everywhere I traced:**
    - Each new write checks `id + version + status='draft'` in the same
  statement (channels, request) or under `FOR UPDATE` (delete).
    - Channels vs approve: if approve holds the row lock, the channels `UPDATE`
  waits, then sees `approved` and gets 0 rows, so it returns 409 before the
  content `DELETE` runs. If channels wins, approve sees a new version and
  returns stale. An approved campaign can't lose its content.
    - Delete vs the content stream's `saveContent`: `saveContent`'s guarded
  `UPDATE` waits on the lock and then finds no row, so it returns errStale. The
  content `INSERT` only runs after that `UPDATE` succeeds, so no orphan rows and
  no FK error. `TestDelete_DuringContentGeneration` confirms this.
    - Delete vs approve: protected by `FOR UPDATE`.
  `TestDelete_ConcurrentWithApprove` would probably catch it if that lock were
  removed, because the final `DELETE` would then remove an approved row.
    - `updateRequest`: the version is checked before the AI call, and the
  `UPDATE` after it is guarded, so a stale answer is never saved (tested with a
  hook while the AI is running).
  - **TOCTOU checks are harmless:** `isResend` reads `parent_campaign_id`, which
  never changes. `editableDraft` is a fast fail only; the guarded write
  decides.
  - **Input validation:** request text goes through `checkRequestText` inside
  `Interpret`, so whitespace or more than 1000 characters returns 422 with no AI
  call (tested). Channels use `checkChannels`. Bodies use `decodeBody` (64 KB,
  unknown fields rejected). The new SQL is fully parameterized.
  - **Routes:** all new routes are on the `api` mux behind `auth.Require` (the
  catalog route has a 401 test). The new routes don't clash with the existing
  patterns.
  - **Status codes follow the existing conventions:** 422 validation, 409 stale
  or wrong state, 404 unknown, 204 delete. Existing endpoints are unchanged; the
  `updateRules` refactor keeps the same message and status.
  - **No real data loss from delete:** a draft cannot be a parent (resend needs
  a completed or cancelled parent) and has no messages.
  - **Catalog drift test:** `TestFields_DescribeWhatValidateAccepts` ties
  `fieldValues` and `fieldOps` to `Validate`.
  - **Sleep replacements:** in the e2e and provider tests, the replacements for
  fixed sleeps really are condition-based and bounded (`shutdownWhen` gives up
  after 5s, `waitUntil` after 10s, the provider key poll after 10s).

  ### Issues

  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **internal/worker/dispatch_test.go, TestProcess_CancelDuringRateLimitWait:
  the watcher can miss the wait, and then the test hangs instead of failing.**
  Verified by reading the code; not reproduced.
     - The limiter is `Every(3s)` and starts empty after `Allow()`. Before the
  reservation, `Tokens()` climbs from 0 towards 1. A reservation at time t sets
  `Tokens() = t/3s − 1`.
     - That value is below −0.5 only if the worker reaches `Wait` within 1.5s.
  Before `Wait`, `Process` does a DB `w.load`, which can be slow on a loaded
  machine.
     - **Failure scenario:** the load takes more than 1.5s, so the watcher never
  sees the reservation. The token arrives at 3s and the send happens.
  `Tokens()` never drops below −0.5 again, so the goroutine spins forever and
  `<-cancelled` blocks. The test hangs until go test's 10-minute timeout, with
  no "machine too slow" message.
     - **Fix:**
       - Use `slow.Tokens() < 0`. It is exact: never true before the
  reservation, and true until the token is due.
       - Give the watcher a deadline.
       - Wait on `<-cancelled` with a `select`/timeout, so the test fails
  cleanly.
     - The lifecycle test's `< -0.5` uses an hour interval, so its window is 30
  minutes and it is fine. I would still use `< 0` there for consistency.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  1. **internal/campaign/http.go, deleteDraft (`DELETE FROM campaigns WHERE id =
  $1`):** this statement is safe only because of the earlier `FOR UPDATE` row
  lock. Adding `AND status = 'draft'` and checking `RowsAffected` would protect
  it if the lock is ever refactored away. Verified safe as written.
  2. **http.go, deleteDraft:** it writes the stale message as a string literal
  instead of `errStale.Error()`, as the other new handlers do. Also, a delete on
  an approved campaign returns "only drafts can be edited" (from
  `editableDraft`). That wording is misleading for a delete, though README
  promises only the 409.
  3. **No concurrency test for channels vs approve,** which the engineer listed
  as a focus area. I verified by reading that it is correct (see Strengths), but
  nothing guards against a regression. A test like
  `TestDelete_ConcurrentWithApprove` that asserts the approved campaign keeps
  both content rows would cover it.
  4. **http.go, updateRequest pre-checks:** three separate queries before the AI
  call (`editableDraft`, `isResend`, `SELECT version, time_zone`). One `SELECT
  status, version, time_zone, parent_campaign_id IS NOT NULL` would do the same
  job. This is simplification only.
  5. **Catalog completeness (rules.go `fieldValues`/`Fields`):** the catalog
  cannot express that `last_order before` takes no `"to"` (this is only a code
  comment). The chip editor will learn it only from a 422 field error. It also
  does not say which limit applies to which field (max_count vs max_amount), and
  it leaves out the 1000-character request limit. D39 needs only operators and
  value types, but the D39 goal ("can't drift") is only partly met for these.
  6. **`audience.Limits` is an exported mutable package map,** and `Fields()`
  returns the `fieldOps` slices without copying them. Any caller could change
  the server's validation rules by mistake. A function that returns a fresh map,
  plus `slices.Clone(ops)`, would be safer.
  7. **TestSend_ConcurrentSameKey:**
     - The "too slow" guard measures when the second request is *sent* (Go
  clock) against the DB `created_at`, a different clock that records the
  transaction start. It does not measure when the second request *arrives*. On a
  very slow machine the test can still fail on the ok/busy assertion instead of
  the clear message.
     - The 2s delay also makes this test slower. Acceptable.

  ### Declined to judge
  - **`PUT /request` clears a send time the marketer set by hand** (via `PUT
  /schedule`) when the new brief names no time. D40 says explicitly that the
  proposed time is replaced, and approval always needs an explicit choice (D29),
  so the effect is small.
  - **AI cost abuse:** parallel `PUT /request` calls with the same version all
  pass the pre-check, so N calls cost N×(up to 2) AI calls, and an "unsupported"
  answer doesn't bump the version, so it can be repeated. `POST /api/campaigns`
  has the same, larger exposure today, and no rate limiting is specified
  anywhere, so this is not a new vector.
  - **DELETE with a JSON body:** some proxies and clients drop DELETE bodies.
  D37 specifies `{version}` in the body, and fetch plus the Vite proxy handle
  it.
  - **The stream's error after a delete is "the campaign changed…; reload",**
  and reloading then gives a 404. D37 accepts this trade-off.
  - **A completed (not cancelled) resend still occupies
  `campaigns_one_active_resend_idx`,** blocking further resends on that parent
  and channel. This predates the change and is D31's scope.
  - **`decodeBody` echoes JSON decoder errors to clients.** This predates the
  change and doesn't expose internals.

  ### Recommendations
  - Fix Important #1 (a few lines).
  - Optionally: add the status guard to the final DELETE, add the
  channels-vs-approve concurrency test, and use `errStale` in deleteDraft.
  - Run the full suite with `-race`, plus lint and the build, before the
  milestone handoff. I did not run these.

  ### Assessment
  **Ready to merge?** With fixes. Only the one small test fix is needed.

  **Reasoning:** The production code matches D37–D40. Every write is checked
  against version and status in the same statement or under a row lock, and the
  approved lifecycle and cancel behavior are untouched. The one real problem is
  a test-sync fix that can still hang under load instead of failing.

  I did not check lint, build, the `-race` runs, or frontend contract details
  beyond the README rows. The only open risk is that the dispatch test can hang
  on a slow CI run.

⏺ The review is back: no critical issues, 1 important and 7 minor. The
  reviewer's verdict is "ready to merge with fixes"; only the one small test fix
  is needed. I checked each finding against the code myself before writing
  this. I haven't changed anything yet.

  Important

  1. The cancel-during-rate-limit test can hang instead of failing. This is in
  my own fix from the last step, internal/worker/dispatch_test.go.
  - Confirmed by working through the math: the test treats a token count below
    −0.5 as "the worker is waiting". With a 3 s token interval, that only works
    if the worker reaches the limiter within 1.5 s. The worker first reads the
    message from the database, and under heavy load that can take longer.
  - What happens then: the watcher never fires, the message is sent when the
    token arrives, and the watcher keeps checking forever. The test then blocks
    until go test's 10-minute timeout, with no clear message.
  - Proposed fix:
    - Check for a token count below 0 instead. That's exact: it can't be true
      before the worker starts waiting, and it stays true until the token is
      due.
    - Give the watcher a deadline, and wait for its result with a timeout, so a
      slow run fails with "machine too slow".
    - Make the same < 0 change in the lifecycle test for consistency. Its
      one-hour interval makes it safe already.

  Minor

  Recommended fixes:
  2. deleteDraft's final DELETE relies only on the earlier row lock. I'll add
  AND status = 'draft' and check the affected row count as a second guard.
  3. deleteDraft wording: it spells out the stale message instead of using the
  shared errStale. Deleting an approved campaign also says "only drafts can be
  edited". I'll use errStale; the status code stays 409.
  4. No concurrency test for channels vs approve. I checked by reading that it's
  correct, but nothing prevents a regression. I'll add a race test asserting
  the approved campaign keeps both content rows.
  5. audience.Limits is an exported map anyone can change, and Fields() returns 
  the server's own operator slices. A caller could change validation by mistake.
  I'll make Limits a function returning a fresh map and copy the slices.

  Recommend leaving these as they are:

  6. Three lookups before the AI call in updateRequest. Merging them is only a
     simplification, and the current helpers keep the same messages as the other
     endpoints.
  7. The catalog can't express that "last order before" takes no end date, or
     which numeric limit applies to which field. D39 only asks for operators and
     value types, and the editor gets a clear field error for these cases. I'd
     add a one-line README note rather than grow the catalog format now.
  8. The provider test's "too slow" check is approximate. It compares the moment
     the second request is sent, on Go's clock, with the database's timestamp.
     On an extremely slow machine it could fail on the normal check instead of
     the clear message, but it can't hang or pass wrongly. I'd leave it.

  Set aside by the reviewer (my view)

  - A re-interpreted brief with no time clears a send time the marketer set by 
    hand. D40 says the proposed time is replaced, and approval always needs an
    explicit choice, so I agree to leave it.
  - AI cost: parallel brief edits each cost an AI call. Creating a draft has the
    same exposure today and there's no rate limit anywhere, so this isn't new.
    It's worth a Known limitations line.
  - A completed resend blocks further resends on the same campaign and channel.
    That predates this work and is D31's scope. It may be a real product gap;
    I'd raise it separately.
  - The other items (DELETE with a body, the stream's message after a delete,
    decoder errors echoed back) I agree to leave as they are.

  Not checked yet

  The reviewer didn't run the race detector, lint or the build. I'll run the
  full suite with -race, plus lint, after the fixes.

  Should I apply fixes 1–5 and add the two README notes (the catalog limit in 7,
  the AI cost line), leaving 6 and 8 as they are? The resend-after-completed
  question would be a separate decision afterwards.

     clear "machine too slow" message instead of hanging for 10 minutes. The
     lifecycle test uses the same condition.

     it stays draft-only even if the row lock is ever refactored away.

     message; it's still a 409.

     of 10 runs even with the protection removed, because the risky order never
     came up.

       change get past its draft check, and commits an approval before releasing
       the lock.

       409, got 200").

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ I need to pause before writing frontend code: PrimeVue 5 is now a commercial,
  license-key-protected product rather than the MIT-licensed library D25
  assumed. I'll check what the last MIT-licensed release requires instead.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ This changes an approved technology decision (D25), so it's your call. The
  facts:

  - PrimeVue 4.5.5 is the last MIT release (8 April 2026). PrimeVue 5.0 (15
    July 2026) moved to the commercial PrimeUI licence. There has been no 4.x
    release since April, so the 4.x line looks frozen.
  - The licence is free for individuals and small teams, but it needs a key with
    yearly renewal. A key is a credential, so I wouldn't commit it. Without
    one, anyone running docker compose up would see PrimeVue's licence notice in
    the app.
  - The rest of the stack is MIT: Vue, vue-router (now 5.x) and Vite. So is Reka
    UI, a headless accessible component library for Vue.

  From the design review: your screens are fully custom-styled (their own
  colours, chips, cards and dialogs). PrimeVue's ready-made look would need
  heavy theming to match them. A headless library supplies only behaviour and
  accessibility, and leaves all the styling to us.

  Options:
  - A. Pin PrimeVue 4.5.5 (MIT): D25 stays as it is. Ready-made tables, dialogs,
    menus, toasts and chips. The catch: it's frozen on a release line with no
    future fixes, and its look must be themed hard to match your design.
  - B. PrimeVue 5 with a Community key: current version, but every reviewer
    either gets a licence notice or needs their own key. Not suitable for a
    deliverable meant to run with one docker compose up.
  - C. Reka UI (MIT, headless) plus our own CSS from your design's colours and 
    spacing (recommended):
    - It still gives us accessible dialogs, dropdown menus, toasts, comboboxes
      and tag inputs, which covers D25's original concern about building
      accessibility ourselves.
    - The styling is ours, so it matches your design directly. Tables become
      plain, semantic HTML tables.
    - The cost: more CSS to write and a D25 update ("Superseded by D41"). It's
      also one more dependency to approve.

  Which option should I use: A, B or C? I'll record the choice in DECISIONS.md
  before scaffolding web/.

  Done so far, not committed yet: the Go part of T15 is written test-first and
  its tests pass. The api serves the built frontend, with page URLs falling back
  to index.html, missing assets returning 404, files outside the site
  unreachable, and only GET/HEAD allowed. /api/ is unaffected. It's switched on
  with a new optional WEB_DIR setting.

  When you answer, I'll also bring the design review: what I'll keep, what I'd
  change, and the backend gaps it reveals. One example of a change: no
  hard-coded demo credentials on the sign-in screen, as your own design notes
  ask, and nothing on the backend exposes a password either. The review will
  also go into the README's UI/UX section.

  ⎿  func login(pool *pgxpool.Pool) http.HandlerFunc {

  Design review. I unpacked your artifact and read all 10 screens. I followed
  the layout, colours, spacing, statuses and the "estimate vs actual" labelling.
  I made three changes:
  - Sign-in: no demo email and password on the page, and no "fill" button. Your
    design notes say not to hard-code them, and the backend never exposes a
    password, so the page points to the README instead.
  - Badge before sign-in: it mentions only the mock providers, because the AI
    mode isn't known until the marketer has signed in.
  - Sidebar: the user menu shows the email instead of a "Marketing" role, since
    everyone has one role (D10). The "+ New campaign" button waits for the
    composer (T16), so it never leads to an empty page.

  Gaps the review found for later tasks:
  - Saving drafts: every edit already saves through the API, so a "Saved"
    indicator may replace the Save button and the unsaved-changes dialog.
  - Campaign names: the design names campaigns, but the backend only stores the
    brief.
  - "Uncertain": in the backend it's a flag on a message, not a separate status.
  - Recipient details: no API lists recipients or their attempt history.
  - 429 counts: the API gives per-channel totals, not "last 5 minutes".
  - Resend: in the backend a resend goes through the normal draft, content and
    approval steps, not a single dialog.

  These are listed as "to settle" in the README and aren't recorded as
  decisions.

  What changed beyond the plan: PrimeVue 5 needs a commercial licence key, so on
  your choice it's replaced by Reka UI plus our own CSS (D41). D25 is marked
  partly superseded.

  What T15 does:
  - The api serves the built app, and every page URL loads it.
  - Sign-in validates fields, shows clear errors and has a show/hide password
    toggle.
  - Pages need a session. A signed-out visitor returns to the page they wanted
    after signing in, and only addresses on this site are accepted, so a crafted
    login link can't send them elsewhere.
  - An expired session sends the marketer back to sign in.
  - The sidebar shows "Mock AI" or "Claude AI" from /api/me, and the user menu
    has log out.
  - Dashboard and Campaigns are labelled placeholders until T16–T18.

  describing how the design was made and is used, and the corrected status line,
  which was out of date.

› Message from general-purpose
  ## T15 review (b480d1c..HEAD, design/ excluded)

  **What I ran:**
  - `web-test`: 23 Vitest tests passed and vue-tsc was clean.
  - `go test ./internal/spa/` passed.
  - I probed the running stack on :8080 with curl.
  - I unpacked the design bundle into the scratchpad and compared the sign-in
  board line by line.
  - I computed WCAG contrast for the main token pairs.

  No files in the repo were changed.

  ### Strengths
  - **LoginView is very close to the sign-in board.** Copy, layout, tokens,
  `role="alert"` error, show/hide toggle, `novalidate` with inline errors, and
  the busy spinner state all match.
  - **The SPA handler is small and correct.**
    - `path.Clean` plus `os.Stat`, so no directory listings.
    - A missing `/assets/*` returns 404 instead of `index.html` served as JS.
    - Immutable cache for hashed assets and `no-cache` for `index.html`.
    - Live checks: `/%2e%2e/etc/passwd` gives 400, `/api/nope` still gives the
  API's JSON 401, `/api` redirects to `/api/`.
    - `/api/` routes keep priority because auth registers a `/api/` catch-all.
  - **The api client is solid.** It turns a network failure or a non-JSON error
  page into a readable `ApiError`, and it excludes `/api/login` and `/api/me`
  from the 401 handler. All of that is tested.
  - **Guard logic is a pure `decide()` with good tests.** I traced the flows;
  none loops: deep link → login → back, signed-in user on `/login`,
  `redirect=/login`, and the `'/'` case with no redirect added.
  - **Open redirect.** `safeRedirect` blocks `//`, `/\`, schemes and arrays. It
  is also defense in depth: navigation only goes through `router.replace`/the
  guard, and `history.replaceState` cannot leave the origin, so the worst case
  is a failed navigation, not an external redirect. No `v-html` anywhere.
  - **Build, test and docs.**
    - Exact dependency pins plus the lockfile; strict tsconfig with
  `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`.
    - Docker npm cache mount.
    - Makefile status propagation is correct: if either suite fails, `make test`
  fails, and the test-db cleanup still runs.
    - D41 and the D25 history note follow the DECISIONS.md rules.
    - The README lists the differences from the design honestly and does not
  oversell.

  ### Issues

  #### Critical
  None.

  #### Important
  1. **The focus ring is too faint for keyboard users** (`web/src/styles.css:33`
  `--focus: 0 0 0 3px rgba(75,68,214,0.35)`).
     - It works out to about #C0BEF1: 1.77:1 on white and 1.75:1 on `--bg`. WCAG
  1.4.11 asks for 3:1 for focus indicators. Every interactive element uses it,
  and the global `:focus-visible` rule removes the outline.
     - The README "Kept throughout: … visible focus" is therefore only weakly
  true.
     - Scenario: a keyboard user tabbing through the sidebar or form can barely
  see where focus is.
     - Fix: use a solid `--primary` 2px outline with an offset, or raise the
  alpha to about 0.7. Verified by calculation.
  2. **Focus is lost after a failed sign-in** (`LoginView.vue`,
  `:disabled="busy"` on both inputs and the button).
     - Pressing Enter in the password field disables the focused control, so the
  browser moves focus to `<body>`. After the 401, focus does not come back.
     - Client-side validation errors (`.field-error`) are not announced either:
  nothing is `aria-live` and focus does not move. A screen-reader user who
  submits empty fields hears nothing.
     - The design has the same pattern, so it is inherited, but the README
  claims "labelled fields with errors next to them".
     - Fix:
       - After a failed submit, call `.focus()` on the first invalid field, or
  on the email field after a server error.
       - Or use `readonly`/`aria-disabled` instead of `disabled` while busy.
     - Traced in code and standard browser behaviour; not tested with a screen
  reader.
  3. **The router wiring is untested.** T15's acceptance criterion ("Vitest:
  auth guard redirect") is met only at the `decide()` level.
     - Untested: the `beforeEach` plus `loadSession` caching (`loaded` reset
  after login, set after logout/`clearSession`) and the unauthorized handler
  pushing to `login?redirect=<current>`.
     - These are the stateful parts where regressions would hide.
     - Fix: one small Vitest file that builds the router with
  `createMemoryHistory` (or exports a factory) and a stubbed `fetch`. Cover:
       - signed out deep link → `/login?redirect=…`
       - login → back to the page
       - a 401 from `/api/campaigns` while on `/campaigns` → login with redirect
       - after logout, `/` → login
     - Verified: no such test exists.

  #### Minor
  4. **`session.ts:13-23` treats a 500 or "unreachable" from `/api/me` as signed
  out and caches it (`loaded=true`).**
     - A signed-in user who hits a transient error on first load lands on the
  sign-in page with no message.
     - If `/api/me` fails right after a successful `/api/login`, `login()`
  returns null, the guard bounces back to `/login`, and the user sees no error
  at all.
     - Consider showing an error when `login()` returns null, and/or not caching
  non-401 failures. Traced, not run.
  5. **Logout with an already-expired session** (`AppLayout.vue:24`).
  `/api/logout` returns 401, which fires `onUnauthorized` (it pushes to
  `login?redirect=…`) at the same time as `signOut`'s `replace`. It ends on
  `/login` correctly because the later navigation wins, but only by race.
  Consider excluding `/api/logout` in `api.ts:41`. Traced.
  6. **No `Content-Security-Policy: frame-ancestors 'none'` (or
  `X-Frame-Options: DENY`) in `spa.go`.** It is one header line and matters once
  there are one-click approve/send screens (clickjacking). For a local demo, a
  proportionate fix is just that header, not a full CSP. Verified absent via
  curl.
  7. **Spinner has no `prefers-reduced-motion` rule** (`styles.css` `.spinner`).
  Verified.
  8. **Accessible names.**
     - The account trigger has `aria-label="Account menu"`, which hides the
  visible name and email from screen readers (WCAG 2.5.3, label in name). Drop
  it or include the name.
     - The mode badge's explanation lives only in `title`, which touch and
  keyboard users never see.
     - The password toggle changes both `aria-label` and `aria-pressed`; use one
  of them with a fixed label.
  9. **Input border `--line-strong` #D9D5CB on white is 1.47:1** (WCAG 1.4.11).
  This comes from the design; labels make it tolerable. Calculated.
  10. **Docs nits.**
      - The `docker-compose.yml` web-test comment says "(make test / make
  lint)", but `make lint` does not run it.
      - The README dev command uses `host.docker.internal`, which needs
  `--add-host=host.docker.internal:host-gateway` on Linux.
      - I did not run the dev command, so I can't confirm it works as written.
  11. **Small design-fidelity gaps.** The alert has no icon, and the toggle
  button has no eye icon. The design's "Signed in. Opening your dashboard…"
  success state is skipped; going straight to the page is fine.
  12. **Unpinned image tag.** `node:22-alpine` is a floating tag; acceptable,
  but pin it like the other images if they are pinned.

  ### Declined to judge
  - **`/metrics` and `/healthz` exposed without auth:** they predate this change
  (D26).
  - **Login CSRF / `Content-Type` enforcement on `POST /api/login`:** backend
  behaviour from T3, not in this range.
  - **The non-Secure cookie:** already recorded as a `shortcut:` in `auth.go`.
  - **Stale session across browser tabs** (logout in tab A, tab B unaware until
  its next 401): expected SPA behaviour and not in the T15 plan.
  - **Expired session not noticed while moving between placeholder pages** that
  make no API calls: this resolves itself once T16–T18 pages fetch data.
  - **Go build layer invalidated when `web/` changes** (`COPY . .` in the Go
  stage): an existing pattern, and the cache mounts keep it cheap.
  - **Full CSP:** not proportionate for a local demo; only `frame-ancestors` is
  suggested (#6).
  - **Bundling the Inter font:** documented in the README as a known limitation.
  - **The documented deviations from the design** (no demo credentials on the
  page, a badge that mentions only the providers before login, email instead of
  role, no "+ New campaign"): I judge all four reasonable and consistent with
  D10/D24 and the design's own notes, so these are not findings.

  ### Recommendations
  - Fix #1–#3 before T16. The focus ring and focus handling will be copied into
  every later screen, and a router-integration test is cheap now.
  - Bundle #5, #6 and #8 as one small follow-up.

  ### Assessment
  **Ready to merge?** With fixes

  **Reasoning:** The functionality, security and build are sound, and every test
  I ran passed. The fixes needed are the AA focus visibility and the
  focus/announcement gaps on the login form, which the README claims are
  handled, plus a router-level test for the stateful guard and 401 wiring.

  Not checked: the README dev command, real-browser or screen-reader behaviour
  (all accessibility points come from reading code and calculating contrast),

⏺ The T15 review is back: no critical issues, 3 important and 9 minor. The
  reviewer's verdict is "ready to merge with fixes". I checked the findings
  against the code. As you asked, I haven't changed anything.

  Important (all confirmed)

  1. The keyboard focus ring is too faint. The focus colour works out to about
     #C0BEF1, roughly 1.8:1 contrast on the white and beige backgrounds. WCAG
     asks for at least 3:1 for focus indicators. Every control uses it, so
     keyboard users can barely see where they are, and the README's "visible
     focus" claim is only weakly true. Fix: a solid 2 px outline in the primary
     colour, with an offset.
  2. Focus is lost after a failed sign-in, and errors aren't announced.
     - The fields and button are disabled while signing in. Pressing Enter
       disables the field you're in, so focus drops to the page body and isn't
       restored after the error.
     - Errors for empty or invalid fields aren't read out by screen readers.
     - Your design has the same pattern, but our README claims accessible
       errors. Fix: after a failed attempt, move focus to the first invalid
       field, or to the email field after a server error.
  3. The router wiring isn't tested. The plan's "auth guard redirect" test
     exists, but only for the pure decision function. The stateful parts aren't
     covered:
     - the guard's cached session check
     - the session reset after login and logout
     - a 401 mid-use sending the marketer to sign-in and back
       Fix: one small test using an in-memory router and a stubbed fetch.

  Minor (confirmed)

  4. A server error on the session check looks like "signed out". If /api/me
     returns 500 or the api is unreachable on first load, the marketer lands on
     sign-in with no message. If it fails right after a successful sign-in,
     they're bounced back to the sign-in page silently.
  5. Logging out with an already-expired session fires two redirects at once. It
     ends on the sign-in page correctly, but only because the second one
     happens to win. Fix: treat a 401 from /api/logout like /api/login.
  6. No clickjacking protection. The served pages could be embedded in another
     site's frame. One frame-ancestors 'none' header would stop that, which
     matters once there are approve and send buttons.
  7. The spinner ignores "reduce motion" settings.
  8. Screen-reader labels:
     - "Account menu" hides the visible name and email from screen readers.
     - The mode badge's explanation is only in a tooltip, which touch and
       keyboard users can't reach.
     - The password toggle changes both its label and its pressed state; one is
       enough.
  9. Input borders are 1.47:1 against white. That comes from your design; the
     visible labels make it tolerable.
  10. Docs:
      - The Compose comment wrongly says make lint runs the frontend checks; it
        doesn't.
      - The dev command relies on host.docker.internal, which on Linux needs an
        extra --add-host flag. I did verify that command on macOS earlier:
        sign-in through the dev server's proxy worked.
  11. Small design gaps: the error alert and the password toggle have no icons.
      The "Signed in, opening your dashboard…" step is skipped; going straight
      to the page is fine.
  12. The node:22-alpine image tag isn't pinned. I'd leave this: the Postgres
      and Go images use floating tags the same way.

  Set aside by the reviewer (I agree)

  The open metrics endpoint, login CSRF, and the non-Secure cookie all predate
  this work. Stale sessions across tabs are normal for this kind of app. A full
  Content-Security-Policy would be out of proportion for a local demo. The
  reviewer also judged all four of my documented changes from your design
  reasonable.

  Proposed next step (not started)

  Fix 1–3 before T16, because the focus style and form pattern will be copied
  into every later screen. Bundle 4–8 and the docs fixes in 10 as one small
  follow-up. Leave 9, 11 and 12 as they are, or polish 11 together with T16.

  The app is still running at http://localhost:8080 for your inspection. Once
  you've looked at the screens, tell me which of these fixes to make.

⏺ I've read the Audience board. Before writing any T16 code I need your decision
  on a few things, because the design assumes things the backend doesn't have.
  Here is my proposed plan.

  T16 plan: composer, step 1 (Audience)

  Pages and routes
  - /campaigns/new: the brief, the channel picker (SMS / Email / Both) and
    Interpret with AI. This calls POST /api/campaigns and opens the new draft.
    The sidebar's "+ New campaign" button appears here.
  - /campaigns/:id: the Audience step for a draft.
    - The step bar from the design: Audience · Content · Review · Sending ·
      Monitor.
    - The brief, with Re-interpret. It asks for confirmation first, because
      hand-edited rules will be replaced (D40).
    - The channel picker. Removing a channel that has content asks for
      confirmation (D38).
    - The rule chips, the summary panel, Delete draft with confirmation (D37),
      and Generate content →, which leads to T17. Until T17 that button is
      disabled with an explanation.

  The rule chip editor (D9)
  - Chips read as plain language, for example "City is İstanbul", "Added to cart
    in the last 30 days (counted from send time)" and "Ordered books since 1
    Oct 2026". The consent chip is fixed and can't be removed.
  - Clicking a chip opens an editor (an accessible Reka dialog) built from GET 
    /api/catalog. Time rules can switch between "last N days" and "since a
    date".
  - + Add rule lists the fields that aren't used yet. Any chip can be removed.
  - Field errors from the server (422) show on the chip they belong to.

  Counts

  The "Estimated audience" panel shows the total and the per-channel counts,
  plus the note that the audience is recalculated when sending starts.

  Unsupported or failed AI answers

  The AI's reason is shown, the brief is kept so the marketer can rephrase it,
  and nothing is saved (D15).

  Tests (Vitest)
  - The chip ↔ rule mapping, both ways, for both time forms (required by the
    plan).
  - The plain-language chip labels.
  - Which fields "+ Add rule" offers.
  - When to ask for confirmation (channel removal, re-interpreting).
  - Turning server field errors into chip errors.

  Also in T16: the visual polish from the review (alert and eye icons).

  Defaults I'd use unless you object. Each one replaces a design element the
  backend can't support:
  - Autosave instead of a Save button. Every chip, channel and schedule change
    already saves through the API with a version check, so the page shows
    "Saving… / Saved" and needs no unsaved-changes dialog. The one exception is
    the brief text: it only takes effect when re-interpreted.
  - No live preview before Apply. Counting rules without saving them would need
    a new endpoint. Instead, Apply saves and the panel shows the change ("+59
    since your last edit").
  - No "built by hand without AI" path for a new draft yet. When the AI can't
    read a brief, the marketer rephrases it. Building rules from scratch could
    come later.
  - Dropped: the design's "‘20% discount’ was used for the content, not the
    audience" note (the backend gives no such explanation), and the "N without
    consent" notes (the counts already exclude anyone without consent).

  One decision first: campaign names

  Your design names every campaign ("Istanbul cart recovery — 20% off"), but the
  backend stores only the brief. The options:
  - A. Recommended: show a title made from the brief, the first sentence cut to
    about 60 characters. No backend change. The downside is that titles read
    like sentences ("Send a 30% discount reminder to users in…").
  - B. Add an editable name, a column plus PUT /api/campaigns/{id}/name with a
    version check. The default comes from the brief, and the composer header has
    the design's inline name field. Proper names on the dashboard and the list,
    but it's backend scope: a migration, an endpoint, tests and a new decision.

  A or B? After that, please confirm the four defaults above, or say which you'd
  change. Then I'll build T16 with TDD, commit it, and not push.

  The T15 fixes are committed, and the app at http://localhost:8080 now runs
  them.

⏺ The review is running. To ground the naming discussion, I'll check how the
  AI's answer format and its validation work today:

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺ Here's my take on AI-generated names. I haven't implemented anything for it.

  Short answer

  Yes, I'd recommend it, with the current first-sentence rule kept as the
  fallback. The AI already reads the brief in one structured call, so asking for
  a name in the same answer costs a few output tokens and no extra round trip.
  And it fixes the weak spot of today's default: names that read like truncated
  sentences ("Send a 30% discount reminder to users in Istanbul who added…"
  versus something like "İstanbul cart reminder · 30% off").

  How it would work

  - Same call: the AI's answer format gets an optional suggested_name, and the
    prompt asks for a short label (at most about 50 characters) in the brief's
    language, without personal data or {{variables}}.
  - Never a reason to fail or retry:
    - The AI's output is untrusted, so the name gets the same checks as a manual
      name: one line, a length limit, no control characters, no {{.
    - A missing or invalid name quietly falls back to the first-sentence
      default.
    - A bad name never makes the interpretation fail, and never triggers the
      extra AI attempt that bad rules get (D15).
    - An unsupported brief creates nothing, so it has no name.
  - Manual edits are kept: today's rule ("rename only while the name equals the
    old brief's default") stops working, because an AI name can't be recomputed
    from the brief. So a flag would record whether the marketer chose the name:
    renaming sets it, and re-interpreting replaces the name only while the flag
    is off.
  - The mock AI: it gives a fixed name for each brief pattern it understands, so
    tests and the demo stay deterministic.
  - Resends keep their parent-based name; no AI is involved.
  - The frontend needs no change: the name already comes from the API and can be
    edited.

  Trade-offs

  ┌──────────────┬──────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────┐
  │              │      AI name, with fallback      │   Current: first     │
  │              │                                  │       sentence       │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │              │ Short and scannable, like the    │ Sentence-like, cut   │
  │ Name quality │ design's examples                │ at about 60          │
  │              │                                  │ characters           │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Cost and     │ A few output tokens, same call   │ None                 │
  │ latency      │                                  │                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │ Reliability  │ Varies slightly between runs in  │ Fully deterministic  │
  │              │ real mode; falls back if invalid │                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │              │ Can describe the intent loosely  │ Literally the        │
  │ Accuracy     │ (for example "VIP customers");   │ marketer's words     │
  │              │ it's only a label, and editable  │                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │              │ Answer format, prompt,           │ Already built and    │
  │ Scope        │ validation, mock names, a flag   │ tested               │
  │              │ column, D42 update, tests        │                      │
  ├──────────────┼──────────────────────────────────┼──────────────────────┤
  │              │ Touches the AI answer format,    │                      │
  │ Risk area    │ the most guarded part of the     │ None                 │
  │              │ system (D15)                     │                      │
  └──────────────┴──────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────┘

  Security: the name comes from the marketer's own brief, has a length limit,
  and is shown as escaped text, so the risk is low. A brief could still nudge
  the AI into an odd name, but the marketer sees it and can rename it.

  Effort: about half a day of backend work, test-driven. The D42 decision gets
  updated with a history note, and the flag needs a new migration.

  Should I implement AI-suggested names this way? If yes, I'll update D42 first,
  then build and test it before T17.

  In the meantime:
  - Flaky test: fixed and committed (088ca1d). The cap now has an optional
    test-only callback that's nil in production. It passed 20 of 20 runs with
    the race detector, the mutation check catches a missing cancel check, and
    the worker package and lint both pass.
  - T16 review: it's running in the background. I'll report its findings,
    checked against the code, before fixing anything.
