› Message from general-purpose
  ## T16 / D42 review (branch milestone-f-frontend, 6bba391..HEAD)

  **Checks I ran:**
  - `web-test`: 88/88 tests pass and `vue-tsc --noEmit` is clean.
  - `go test ./internal/campaign/`: ok (16.7s). The test DB was removed
  afterwards.
  - Two throwaway Playwright scripts against the running app, in
  `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign-composer/b26c288a-6558-4a4
  0-b2cd-ebdb9a1b6414/scratchpad/review-t16/` (`script.mjs` and `script2.mjs`).
  - These flows worked in the browser: re-interpret with the default-name
  rename, the rule editor (Escape returns focus to the chip, Apply adds a city),
  and delete through its dialog (redirects, then the draft returns 404).
  - **Side effect:** testing created drafts 10, 11 and 12 in the dev DB. I
  deleted 12 through the UI; 10 and 11 are still there.

  ### Strengths
  - `createSaver` is small and correct for what it does: one save at a time, the
  version read when each save runs, a 409 locks the page, and an ordinary error
  does not block the next save. Its tests cover all three.
  - The rule ↔ form mapping is pure and well tested, including both time forms,
  a cleared end date, and never sending an end date for "last order before".
  This is the test the plan requires.
  - Names: the server checks are strict (code points, control characters,
  trimmed). The default-name rule lives only in Go, the empty stored name falls
  back to the default, and the rename-on-re-interpret uses a version-checked
  `CASE`. Tests cover Turkish text, `10.000`, a long resend name, a stale
  version and approved campaigns.
  - Security: there is no `v-html` anywhere and all user text is rendered by
  interpolation. The new route goes through the existing session guard, and SQL
  is parameterized (the `name = $4` fragment is a constant).
  - Accessibility basics are in place: Reka dialogs with focus trap and Escape,
  a labelled name input, `aria-pressed` on the channel buttons, chip remove
  buttons named after their rule, `role=alert` on errors, a step bar with
  `aria-current="step"`, and a permanent polite status region.
  - The docs match the code, and the design differences are recorded in the
  README.

  ### Issues

  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  1. **Removing a channel that has content never works; the confirm button
  crashes.**
     - Where: `web/src/views/CampaignView.vue:299`
  (`@confirm="applyChannels(channelConfirm!.next)"`) together with
  `ConfirmDialog.vue:20,28`.
     - Cause: Reka's `AlertDialogAction` runs its own click handler first
  (`onOpenChange(false)` → `update:open` → `emit('close')`), which sets
  `channelConfirm = null` synchronously. Your `@click="emit('confirm')"` runs
  after that, so `channelConfirm!.next` throws `TypeError: Cannot read
  properties of null (reading 'next')`.
     - Effect: going from Both to SMS on a draft with content and clicking
  "Remove and delete content" closes the dialog and changes nothing. The
  channels stay `["sms","email"]` with no message to the marketer. This breaks
  the D38 flow.
     - **Verified:** ran it in Playwright; the TypeError appeared in the console
  and the API still showed both channels.
     - Fix: give the confirm event the data (`@confirm="applyChannels(next)"`
  from a captured local), or have ConfirmDialog emit `confirm` before closing.
  Re-interpret and delete are not affected because their handlers read no dialog
  state.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  2. **Quick rule edits overwrite each other, so a removed rule comes back.**
     - Where: `CampaignView.vue:121-135` (`applyRule` and `removeRule` build the
  new rule list when clicked, not when the save runs).
     - Cause: the queue sends the latest version but an old copy of the rules,
  so the version check (D10) cannot catch it. The chips are only disabled while
  locked or while the brief is being re-read (`:258`), not while a save is
  running.
     - Scenario: a marketer quickly removes "No order…" then "Added to cart…".
  Both saves succeed (v1→v3), but the second one sends a list that still has "No
  order", so that rule reappears.
     - **Verified:** in Playwright the final rules were city plus no_order, and
  the UI showed the same.
     - Same pattern: Escape closes the editor while it is still saving, then the
  marketer removes a chip.
     - Fix (smallest): build `next` inside the save callback from
  `campaign.value.rules` at run time. For removal, find the rule by `field op`
  key, not by index. Alternatively, disable the chips and "+ Add rule" while
  saving.
     - Add a test in the style of `saver.test.ts` showing a queued save sees the
  state left by the previous one.
  3. **Moving between two campaigns reuses the page and keeps the old campaign's
  state.**
     - Where: `CampaignView.vue:55` (`watch(id, load)` resets only `campaign`
  and `loadError`); `AppLayout.vue:64` has an unkeyed `<RouterView />`.
     - Scenario: from draft 11, "+ New campaign" creates 12 (route replaced).
  Then Back goes straight to `/campaigns/11` with the same page instance.
     - What carries over: the `change` badge ("−11 since your last edit" from
  campaign 12, shown on 11), the saver state and its queue (a conflict would
  lock 11), `staleContent`, `briefProblem`, `saveError`, `notice` and `editor`.
     - A save still queued at navigation calls `campaigns.setX(id.value, …)` and
  `id.value` is read when it runs, so it can write to the newly opened
  campaign.
     - **Verified:** the carried-over badge (Playwright). Writing into the other
  campaign is traced in the code, not run.
     - Fix: key the page on the id (`<RouterView :key="$route.params.id ??
  $route.name" />`, or wrap CampaignView in a keyed component) so each campaign
  gets a fresh page and saver. Capture `id` when a save is queued.
  4. **A failed rule save can look like success, or show no message at all.**
     - Where: `CampaignView.vue:104-118` and `:132-135`.
     - Case 1: `saveRules` returns only the target rule's errors plus list-wide
  ones. If the server rejects with field errors that belong to other rules, it
  returns `[]`.
       - `applyRule` then closes the editor as if the edit was saved (`:129`).
       - `removeRule` shows nothing.
       - Only the small "Not saved" label remains.
     - Case 2: if the marketer closes the editor while it is saving and the save
  fails, the error lands on a closed editor and is lost. Rule saves turn off
  the banner (`banner=false`).
     - Case 3: when one save finishes, `editor.value = null` closes whatever
  editor is open by then, possibly one opened afterwards.
     - `ruleErrorMap` is never filled, so the per-chip error display in
  `RuleChips.vue:18-32` cannot run.
     - **Plausible, traced:** today the stored rules are server-validated, so
  case 1 is unlikely. Cases 2 and 3 need Escape during the save.
     - Fix:
       - Fill `ruleErrorMap` from `byRule`.
       - If no message is left for the target, show all of them.
       - Only close the editor if `editor.value === e`.
       - Show the error in `rulesProblem` when the editor is already closed.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  5. `CampaignView.vue:144-177`: `reinterpret` and `deleteDraft` call
  `saver.save` directly, so an old `saveError` banner from an earlier failure
  stays visible after they succeed. Clear `saveError` at the start, or use
  `save()`. Traced.
  6. `AudiencePanel.vue:25`: "since your last edit" keeps showing the rules or
  channels change after a later rename or brief edit, and an equal count (`0`)
  hides it. The change line is mounted with `role=status` already filled in,
  which screen readers often do not announce; the permanent `notice` region
  covers the saves, but the count change is not announced. Plausible.
  7. `RuleChips.vue:25-26`: the last rule's remove button is `disabled` with the
  reason only in `title`. Disabled buttons cannot be focused and `title` is not
  reliably read, so keyboard and screen-reader users never hear why. Use
  `aria-disabled` with visible or `aria-describedby` text, or hide the button.
  8. Names:
     - `NameField.vue:17` does not check for control characters. A pasted tab
  gets the server's 422 ("…on one line") in the page banner, while the input
  keeps the bad value with no error on the field itself (verified).
     - `checkName` (`internal/campaign/name.go:66-72`) allows format characters
  (bidi overrides U+202E, zero-width) and U+2028. There is no XSS risk (Vue
  escapes text), but these allow list spoofing.
  9. `defaultName` treats "e.g. " or "Dr. " as the end of a sentence. This is
  cosmetic, and only the default name is affected.
  10. Re-interpret (`CampaignView.vue:225`) has no character counter or
  1000-character limit, unlike New campaign. Too long a brief gets the server's
  422 message, which is acceptable.
  11. Design fidelity:
      - The rule editor is a modal dialog, not the popover attached to the chip
  in board 04. This is reasonable for accessibility but is not in the README's
  list of deviations.
      - The breadcrumb "Campaigns / New campaign" is not shown.
      - The help text says "Suggested by the AI" even after manual edits.

  ### Tests
  - The pure logic is well covered.
  - `CampaignView` has no automated test. The three highest-risk bugs above all
  live in that untested component code, which is why tests missed them.
  - Two tests can be added without new dependencies (no jsdom or test-utils is
  installed):
    1. Move "what to save" into small pure helpers (for example
  `withRuleReplaced(rules, key, rule)` and `withoutRule(rules, key)`) and test
  that the payload is built when the save runs: two queued removals end with
  both rules gone.
    2. Test that ConfirmDialog's confirm does not depend on parent state cleared
  on close, by passing the data explicitly. A real DOM-level dialog test would
  need `@vue/test-utils` plus jsdom, which is a new dependency and needs
  approval. Alternatively, keep a Playwright smoke script outside the repo as
  part of the release checklist.

  ### Declined to judge
  - The approved defaults (autosave, no preview before Apply, no manual-rules
  path for new campaigns, the two dropped notes): already approved.
  - Campaign list, content step and dashboard placeholders: T17/T18.
  - The `windowCap.waiting` test hook in production code: outside the T16 focus,
  and harmless (nil in production).
  - Whether a 409 for "only drafts can be edited" (approved in another tab)
  should say "changed elsewhere": the wording is acceptable and the outcome
  (reload) is right.
  - No ordering or debounce for typing in the name: saving on blur or Enter is
  the documented behaviour.
  - Resend name suffix wording ("· resend (SMS)"): already recorded in D42.
  - The 1000-character brief limit and the time-zone source (browser IANA zone):
  earlier decisions (D15) and existing behaviour.
  - Pixel-level design fidelity (spacing, colours): not checked beyond structure
  and wording.

  ### Recommendations
  1. Fix #1, then rerun the channel-removal flow in a browser.
  2. Fix #2 by building rule payloads inside the save callback, and add the
  pure-helper test.
  3. Fix #3 by keying the page on the campaign id.
  4. Tidy #4 (fill `ruleErrorMap`, guard the editor close).
  5. The rest can wait.

  ### Assessment — Ready to merge? **With fixes**

  composer page, though, the D38 channel-removal confirmation does nothing
  (verified crash), quick rule edits overwrite each other (verified), and state

  merge.

  I did not check pixel-level design fidelity, screen-reader output (only DOM
  and ARIA were inspected), or the real-AI mode. The main risk is drafts 10 and
  11 left in the dev DB.

  T16 review: findings still open

  Critical
  1. Removing a channel that has content doesn't work. I wrote ConfirmDialog so
     that the dialog's "closed" event clears the page's pending channel change.
     Reka fires that event before "confirm", so the confirm handler reads
     cleared state and throws a TypeError, and the channels stay unchanged with
     no message. The reviewer reproduced this in a browser. My smoke test missed
     it because it only removed a channel that had no content. The re-interpret
     and delete confirmations are unaffected.

  Important

  2. Quick rule edits overwrite each other. Removing two chips quickly brings
     the first one back. Each edit builds its new rule list when it's clicked,
     not when its save runs, so the second save sends a stale list with a valid
     version. Reproduced in a browser.
  3. State carries over between campaigns. Moving from one draft to another
     reuses the same page: the "since your last edit" change, the save queue's
     state and error banners carry over. A save still queued at that moment
     could write to the newly opened campaign. Fix: give each campaign its own
     page instance.
  4. A failed rule save can look like success, or show nothing:
     - If the server reports a problem on a different rule, the editor closes as
       if the edit had worked.
     - If the editor was closed during the save, its error is lost.
     - A finished save can close an editor opened after it.
     - Per-chip error display exists but is never filled in.

  Minor

  5. An old error banner stays visible after a later re-interpret or delete
     succeeds.
  6. "Since your last edit" goes stale after unrelated changes and isn't
     announced to screen readers.
  7. The last rule's disabled remove button hides its reason ("a campaign needs
     at least one rule") from keyboard and screen-reader users.
  8. The re-interpret box has no 1,000-character counter, unlike New campaign.
  9. Undocumented design deviations: the rule editor is a modal dialog rather
     than a popover, there's no breadcrumb, and the help text still says
     "Suggested by the AI" after manual edits.

  Tests: the composer page has no automated test, and that's where bugs 1–3 are.
  Without new dependencies, I'd move "what to save" into small pure functions
  that build the rule list when the save actually runs, and test that two queued
  removals both stick. Testing the dialogs themselves would need a DOM test
  library (@vue/test-utils with jsdom), a new dependency that needs your
  approval. The alternative is to keep the Playwright smoke script outside the
  repo and run it before each milestone.

  Verdict: "ready to merge with fixes". The reviewer judged the backend and the
  pure logic solid, and said issues 1–3 must be fixed first.

⏺ The T16 review fixes are done and verified. They're committed in 3 commits on
  milestone-f-frontend: 3a71d79, c6f6bf3 and c9b5781. Nothing is pushed or
  merged.

  What I fixed

  1. Removing a channel that has content works again. The confirmation dialog
     now runs the action before it closes; previously it closed first and the
     action found nothing to apply.
  2. Quick edits can't undo each other. Each rule save builds its list at the
     moment it runs, from the latest saved rules, and finds rules by field and
     operator instead of position. There are 6 new tests, including two quick
     queued removals and an edit followed by a removal.
  3. No state carries over between campaigns. Each campaign gets its own page
     instance, so the campaign id, the save queue and all messages belong to
     that campaign only.
  4. A failed save is never shown as success:
     - Server errors appear on the chip they belong to.
     - If the problem is on another rule, the editor says so instead of closing.
     - If the editor was closed during the save, the error shows on the page.
     - A finished save only closes its own editor.
  5. Old error banners clear when a re-interpret or delete starts.
  6. The last rule's remove button stays focusable and has a visible reason: "A
     campaign needs at least one audience rule."

⏺ I couldn't find our TR/EN discussion anywhere: not in DECISIONS.md, the plan,
  CASE.md, or the session exports 01–05. So I'd rather confirm the design than
  guess. Your requirements are clear:
  - English by default, with a language switcher that remembers the choice
  - localized UI text, errors, statuses, numbers and dates
  - briefs, customer data and AI-generated content are never translated or
    changed

  Two implementation choices need your approval, because one adds a dependency
  and the other affects how errors work.

  1. The i18n library

  - A. Recommended: our own small module, no dependency. Two typed dictionaries,
    en and tr; a test makes sure both have exactly the same keys. {name}
    placeholders, a simple plural rule, and the browser's built-in Intl for
    numbers and dates (10,000 vs 10.000, "1 Oct 2026" vs "1 Eki 2026"). The
    language is stored in localStorage, defaults to English, and sets <html 
    lang>. About 80 lines plus the dictionaries.
  - B. vue-i18n, the standard library. Richer features (pluralization rules,
    lazy loading, component interpolation), but it's a new dependency for two
    languages and a handful of screens.

  2. Error messages

  Today the backend sends English sentences (for example "the campaign changed
  since you loaded it…"), and the UI shows them as they are.
  - A. Recommended: the frontend localizes by situation. Each screen maps the
    cases it knows to Turkish or English text:
    - wrong password (401)
    - changed elsewhere (409)
    - not found (404)
    - server unreachable
    - AI unavailable or timed out (502/504)
    - unexpected server error (5xx)

    Client-side checks (rule editor, sign-in, brief length) are fully localized.
    Server validation details that the UI can't anticipate are shown in their
    original English under a localized heading, so nothing is lost. The AI's 
    "unsupported" reason is AI-generated content, so it's shown exactly as 
    written, under a localized label.
  - B. Stable error codes from the backend (for example {"error": "...", "code":
    "stale_version"}), mapped in the frontend. It's cleaner long-term, but it
    touches every handler and many tests just before T17.

  What stays untouched

  Briefs, customer and seed data (names, cities, category codes) and AI output
  (rules as the AI wrote them, message content, unsupported reasons) are never
  translated or changed. Only the UI around them is localized; for example, the
  chip "City is İstanbul" becomes "Şehir: İstanbul", with the city name
  unchanged.

  How it would be delivered

  - Decision: recorded as D44.
  - Docs: a README section on how the language works and how to add text.
  - Tests: dictionary parity, placeholders and plurals, Turkish number and date
    formatting, Turkish chip labels, error mapping, and saving the chosen
    language.
  - Browser check: a Playwright run switching languages on the sign-in and
    composer screens.

  Do you approve 1A (our own module) and 2A (frontend localizes by situation)?
  If you'd prefer vue-i18n or backend error codes, I'll plan for that instead.

✻ Churned for 37s · done 12:20 AM

› Message from general-purpose
  ## Review: D44 English/Turkish UI (c9b5781..9aae7a1, branch
  milestone-f-frontend)

  **What I ran:** the full `web-test` run. vue-tsc passed and all 149 tests in
  13 files passed. I also ran a scanner probe
  (`/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign-composer/b26c288a-6558-4a
  40-b2cd-ebdb9a1b6414/scratchpad/review-i18n/cov.mjs`) and a live Playwright
  run in Turkish (`.../review-i18n/live.mjs`). The live run created campaign 51
  from a Turkish brief and then deleted it (204). No repo files were changed.

  ### Strengths
  - The translator is small and does its job. The `tr: Record<Key, Message>`
  type catches missing or extra keys. A test checks that both dictionaries use
  the same placeholders. Plural selection is safe: an undefined or NaN count
  falls back to "other". Storage failures are handled and tested. `document` is
  guarded.
  - No captured-string reactivity bugs. Messages are stored as errors, keys or
  functions (`Text = () => string`, `problem.text`, `briefProblem.err`, `notice:
  Key`), and everything else uses `computed`/`t()` in templates. Live check:
  switching TR→EN on the campaign page re-rendered the chips, the footer, the
  tab title and `<html lang>`.
  - Data is preserved. The live run showed the AI rule value `books` unchanged
  inside a Turkish chip, and the brief title and text unchanged. `aiReason`
  returns the AI text as written and is tested with a Turkish reason under the
  EN UI. Dates are formatted as UTC.
  - `capitalize` uses the locale ("i"→"İ" in Turkish). I read every chip
  template in en.ts and tr.ts: none starts with a data placeholder, so today it
  never changes a city or category.
  - The language switcher marks each language name with its own `lang`, uses
  `aria-pressed`, and the group label is translated.

  ### Issues

  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None.

  #### Important (Should Fix)
  1. **The Turkish UI uses "taslak" for both "brief" and "draft"**
  (web/src/i18n/tr.ts:27, 86, 91–97, 105–108, 123, 128 versus 59, 100, 127,
  130).
     - "Brief" became "taslak", which is also the word for draft, used in the
  status badge, delete and keep buttons.
     - On one screen you get "Kampanya taslağı" (the brief), a "Taslak" badge
  (draft status), "Taslağı yeniden yorumla" (re-interpret the brief) and
  "Taslağı sil…" (delete the draft).
     - The delete dialog, seen live: "Taslak silinsin mi? | … Taslak metni,
  hedef kitle kuralları … Bu taslaktan hiçbir şey gönderilmedi." Here "taslak"
  means the brief in one sentence and the draft in the next.
     - Why it matters: a marketer cannot tell whether "Taslağı yeniden yorumla"
  or "Taslak değişmedi" refers to the text or the campaign, right next to a
  destructive action.
     - Fix: use a separate term for brief everywhere it means brief, such as
  "kampanya açıklaması" or "brif" (e.g. "Açıklamayı yeniden yorumla", "Taslak
  metni" → "Açıklama"). Keep "taslak" only for draft.
     - Verified live.

  2. **`errorText` turns every 409 into "This draft was changed elsewhere…
  Reload"** (web/src/i18n/errors.ts:17–18).
     - The backend uses 409 for several different things: version conflicts,
  "only drafts can be edited/approved", "the audience is fixed once sending has
  started", "no recipients were skipped for the character limit on this
  channel", "this campaign already has an active resend on this channel", "only
  approved or sending campaigns can be cancelled"
  (internal/campaign/approve.go:133–366, http.go:342/447).
     - README step 2 tells future screens to use `errorText(err)`. A T17/T18
  resend screen would then tell the marketer the draft changed elsewhere when
  the real reason is "no recipients were skipped", and the server's message is
  dropped.
     - Fix: map 409 to `error.changed` only for the version conflict (the
  saver's conflict state, or the stale message). Treat other 409s like
  unforeseen validation: localized heading plus the server's details.
  Alternatively let callers pass per-endpoint keys.
     - Traced, not run. The current screens mostly hit the stale case, so this
  is latent.

  3. **`coverage.test.ts` misses common patterns, and the docs claim more than
  it does** (web/src/i18n/coverage.test.ts:9–20; README "make test fails if…";
  D44 "A test fails when a component shows text that doesn't come from the
  dictionaries").
     - The probe showed these all pass the scan:
       - `{{ busy ? 'Saving…' : 'Save' }}` (string literals inside mustaches)
       - `:aria-label="'Close dialog'"` (bound attribute literals)
       - `v-text="'…'"`
       - one- or two-word script strings (`'Saving…'`, `'Not saved'`)
       - strings with an apostrophe in the first word (`"Can't reach the
  server."`)
       - lowercase or Turkish-initial strings
       - single-quoted static attributes (`placeholder='…'`)
       - `aria-description`
     - It also never scans `.ts` files (rules.ts, saver.ts, api.ts), where UI
  text has already lived.
     - False positives: `&nbsp;` is flagged.
     - Why it matters: this is meant to be the guard for future screens, and the
  ternary/bound-literal patterns are exactly what new screens tend to use.
     - Fix:
       - Also run a literal check over mustache contents, bound attribute values
  and `v-text`.
       - Accept any letter-containing literal of 2+ characters, with a small
  allowlist for codes and keys.
       - Include `'` in the first-word class.
       - Add a self-test with known-bad samples so the scanner can't silently
  regress.
       - Soften the README/D44 wording until then.
     - Verified with the probe.

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)
  4. **Wrong error message when the session can't load after sign-in**
  (web/src/session.ts:38).
     - If login succeeds but `/api/me` then returns 401 (e.g. the cookie is
  blocked), `new ApiError('session could not be loaded', 0)` has status 0, so
  the user sees "Can't reach the server. Check that it is running."
     - The intended key `error.sessionLoad` is now unused (my key scan found it
  as the only dead key). This is a regression from the old specific message.
     - Fix: give it a status or code that maps to `t('error.sessionLoad')`.
     - Traced.

  5. **Raw server English on the rule chip**
  (web/src/components/RuleChips.vue:35).
     - The chip error shows the server's English with no localized heading. The
  editor and the alert do add the heading.
     - Fix: prefix the heading, or use the `details()` wrapper.
     - Traced.

  6. **English server details inside Turkish text have no `lang="en"`**
  (errors.ts:25, CampaignView.vue:145).
     - The details are joined into one string, so a Turkish screen reader voice
  reads the English details with Turkish pronunciation.
     - Fix: return the heading and the details separately and render the details
  in `<span lang="en">`.
     - Plausible.

  7. **`capitalize()` is applied to the whole chip label** (rules.ts:62).
     - Safe today (see Strengths), but a future Turkish template that starts
  with `{list}` would capitalize a lowercase category code, which alters data.
  The unknown-field fallback `${r.field} ${r.op}` also gets capitalized.
     - Fix: capitalize only the window phrase when it leads, or write the
  capital in the dictionary entries.
     - Latent.

  8. **Wording issues.**
     - Turkish:
       - `mode.mock` "Sahte" reads as "fake/fraudulent"; "Simüle" or "Test" is
  better.
       - `rule.lastOrderAfter` with a date gives "Son siparişini 1 Eki 2026
  tarihinden beri verdi"; "tarihinden sonra" is better.
       - `rule.lastOrderBeforeDate` has no verb.
       - The channel name "E-posta" stays capitalized mid-sentence ("SMS ve
  E-posta mesajlarını", seen live).
     - English:
       - "SMS and Email messages" and "because the Email was too long": "Email"
  capitalized mid-sentence.
       - `campaign.notEditable` produces "This campaign is Approved/Sending".
       - `rules.anotherRule` wraps a full sentence and heading inside another
  one, which reads awkwardly.

  9. **Formatting edge cases.**
     - `formatDate('')` (a rule with no `from`) shows "1 Jan 1900", because
  `Date.UTC(0, …)` maps year 0 to 1900. Invalid data only; showing "—" would be
  better. Traced.
     - `composer.count` doesn't locale-format its numbers ("1000" rather than
  "1.000" in Turkish).

  10. **Doc wording** (D44 and README): "rules as the AI wrote them … shown
  exactly as stored" contradicts the chips, which translate how a rule is
  phrased. Say "rule values (cities, categories, numbers, dates) are never
  changed".

  11. **Test gaps.**
      - No component or route test that a locale switch re-renders text or
  updates the tab title (only the smoke script checks it).
      - No Turkish dotted-i capitalize test.
      - No plural test for NaN or non-integer counts.
      - No scanner self-test (see 3).
      - No `errorText` test for 400 on login, or for the 409 variants.

  ### Declined to judge
  - The brief `.trim()` before sending (NewCampaignView, CampaignView): it
  predates this diff.
  - Brief truncation in `briefTitle`: approved by D43.
  - Whether data spans (brief, cities, categories, AI reason) should carry
  `lang`: their language is unknown, and guessing would be wrong.
  - Showing seed category codes like `books` untranslated in a Turkish chip:
  required by the "never translate data" rule.
  - Storing the language per account: rejected in D44.
  - vue-i18n and backend error codes: rejected in D44.
  - Live-region re-announcement in the new language after a switch: harmless,
  not covered by the requirements.
  - No "session expired" message on the login page after a redirect: existing
  behavior.
  - A new `Intl.PluralRules` object on every `t()` call: negligible at this
  size.
  - `name in params` matching prototype keys like `{constructor}`: no dictionary
  uses such a placeholder.
  - The English tab title shown for a moment before the router's first
  navigation in Turkish.

  ### Recommendations
  - Fix 1 (terminology) and 2 (409 mapping) before T17/T18 build on `errorText`.
  - Make 3 a real guard, with a self-test, before more screens arrive.
  - Add `error.sessionLoad` back to its path (4).
  - Return errors as `{ heading, details }` so the details can carry `lang="en"`
  (5, 6).

  ### Assessment
  **Ready to merge? With fixes.** The translator, reactivity and data handling
  are correct, and the type check and all 149 tests pass. The Turkish
  brief/draft term clash is a real usability problem on the main screen. The 409
  mapping and the leaky coverage guard would mislead the screens that come
  next.

  Not checked: `<title>`/SSR behavior outside Vite, and the 401→login redirect
  in Turkish.

  i18n review: what it found and what I fixed

  The reviewer found no critical issues and gave "ready to merge with fixes". I
  fixed the three important findings, each with tests:
  1. Turkish used "taslak" for both the brief and the draft, right next to
     "delete draft". The brief is now "açıklama" ("Kampanya açıklaması",
     "Açıklamayı yeniden yorumla"), and "taslak" means only the draft. A test
     enforces this for every dictionary entry.
  2. Every 409 was shown as "changed elsewhere", but the backend also uses 409
     for "no recipients were skipped", "already has an active resend" and
     similar. Now the server's reason is kept, and only the composer's own
     conflict state says "changed elsewhere". That matters for the T17/T18
     resend and approve screens.
  3. The coverage scan missed common patterns. It now also checks literals
     inside template expressions, user-facing bound attributes, v-text and every
     .ts module. A self-test checks it against 17 known good and bad samples.
     Developer-only messages are marked i18n-ignore, and the docs describe the
     scan honestly as a heuristic.

  I also fixed two smaller ones:
  - Session-load message: a session that can't load after sign-in showed "can't
    reach the server" again (a regression I'd introduced); it has its own
    message back.
  - D44 and README wording: rule chips phrase a rule in the UI language, but its
    values never change.

  Deferred minor findings

  - server-error details have no lang="en" marker, and the chip error lacks its
    heading
  - capitalisation only happens to be safe in today's templates
  - Turkish and English wording: "Sahte" may read as "fake"; "E-posta" and
    "Email" are capitalised mid-sentence; "This campaign is Approved"
  - an empty date shows "1 Jan 1900"
  - the brief counter isn't locale-formatted
  - no automated test that switching language re-renders the page

  I'd fix the wording and formatting items while building the T17/T18 screens.

  Before T17

  CLAUDE.md requires a plan you approve before major work, so here's the T17
  outline. It covers content and review, following design boards "2 · Content"
  and "3 · Review & approve":

  Content step
  - The AI writes the messages and streams them into editable fields, with an
    indicator that it's writing.
  - Variables can be inserted, and the content can be regenerated or stopped.
  - Edits save automatically, like the Audience step, and the D40 warning that
    content may not match a re-interpreted brief shows there.

  Message checks
  - The SMS counter shows GSM-7/UCS-2 encoding and segment counts.
  - Per channel, it shows how many messages would be over the limit for this
    audience, from the existing estimate endpoint.

  Review and approve
  - Rules, content and estimates on one page.
  - An approval dialog with an explicit "Send now" or "Schedule" choice, in the
    local time zone and sent as UTC, plus "I've checked" and a second
    confirmation.
  - A send time suggested from the brief is preselected as "Suggested from your
    brief"; an unusable suggestion leaves both options unselected.

  Tests
  - Vitest for the plan's required cases: smsInfo, the stream parser across
    chunk boundaries, and the suggestion prefill.
  - Playwright for the full flow.

  One open decision: the message preview. The design previews the message with a
  real recipient's details ("Shown as Ayşe Demir", "longest name in this
  group…"). No API returns sample recipients today. Options:
  - A. Recommended: a fixed fictional sample (for example "Ayşe Yılmaz,
    İstanbul") plus the over-limit counts. No new endpoint, and no customer data
    on screen.
  - B. A small endpoint returning a few real audience members, including the
    longest name. It's more realistic, but it shows personal data in the UI and
    adds backend scope.

  Do you approve this T17 plan with preview option A, or would you prefer B?

› Message from general-purpose
  ## T17 review: content step and review & approve (branch milestone-f-frontend,
  01425c3..58f0bfb)

  **What I ran**
  - `docker compose --profile test run --rm web-test` passed: 21 files, 243
  tests, exit 0.
  - Two Playwright probe scripts against the running app:
    - /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign-composer/b26c288a-6558-
  4a40-b2cd-ebdb9a1b6414/scratchpad/review-t17/r1.mjs
    - /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign-composer/b26c288a-6558-
  4a40-b2cd-ebdb9a1b6414/scratchpad/review-t17/r2.mjs
  - Cleanup: campaigns 88 and 89 deleted, 87 cancelled (it was scheduled 3 days
  ahead, never "now"). I checked this over the API.
  - I did not run the Go tests, because T17 changed no backend code.

  ### Strengths
  - **The approval guarantee holds.** The approve call sends the version loaded
  on Review (ReviewView.vue:95). The server compares versions under a row lock
  (approve.go:124–138). So the marketer always approves exactly the content and
  rules shown on that page. I confirmed this: content changed elsewhere while
  Review was open gave a 409 and the campaign stayed a draft.
  - **Approval is guarded at every step:**
    - `approve()` closes the dialog first and has a `busy` guard, so a double
  click or a second confirm does nothing.
    - When the dialog opens, focus is on "Go back", so pressing Enter cancels.
    - The confirm button is a plain button rather than AlertDialogAction, so the
  parent never acts on cleared state.
    - The approve button checks the blockers on click, not just through
  `aria-disabled`.
    - The server rejects a missing `send`.
    - A suggested time is preselected only if it is still usable. The review
  tick and the dialog are still required.
  - **Streaming handles the edge cases.** The SSE parser copes with chunk
  splits, CRLF, comments and bad JSON. TextDecoder uses `stream: true`. Abort is
  passed through as AbortError. `retry` clears the live text. Live text is
  display-only (`shown()`), so it is never saved; the editors refill from a
  refetch only after `done`. Generation runs inside the save queue, so a
  blur-save queued earlier runs first and the stream gets the new version.
  - **Other checks:**
    - The GSM-7 basic table is exactly the 127 GSM 03.38 characters (verified
  with a script).
    - Segment counts 160/153 and 70/67 are right, and the 160 limit counts code
  points (D2).
    - `render` matches the backend's `content.Render`: same order, same space
  collapsing and trimming.
    - Message text is shown as plain text interpolation, never as HTML.
    - Turkish works on the review screen.

  ### Issues
  #### Critical (Must Fix)
  None found.

  #### Important (Should Fix)

  **1. Hand-typed text is replaced by the AI with no confirmation.**
  - Where: ContentView.vue:107–110.
  - What's wrong: `askToWrite` only asks for confirmation when *saved* content
  exists (`campaign.value?.content`).
  - Scenario: a marketer types the SMS while the email is still empty, so
  nothing autosaves (the content is incomplete). They then click "Write with
  AI". There is no dialog and their text is replaced.
  - Fix: also ask for confirmation when the editors are not empty, for example
  when `!unchanged()` or when any chosen field has text.
  - Verified (r1, step 1: "dialog shown? false", field overwritten).

  **2. Edits are lost when the marketer leaves without the field losing focus.**
  - Where: ContentView.vue:75–97 and MessageField.vue:55,67.
  - What's wrong: saving happens only on blur. There is no `onBeforeRouteLeave`
  flush and no `beforeunload` warning.
  - Scenario: the marketer types, then uses browser Back (Alt+←, Cmd+[ or the
  swipe gesture), closes the tab, or reloads. The edit is gone without any
  message.
  - What works: clicking a link with the mouse saves first (blur fires on
  mousedown). I checked both the step link and "Review & approve →", and Review
  showed the edit.
  - Fix: in `onBeforeRouteLeave`, run `await commit()`, and block leaving if the
  content is incomplete or the save fails. Add a `beforeunload` prompt while
  there are unsaved changes. Optionally also autosave after a pause in typing.
  - Verified (r1, step 3: the saved SMS was unchanged after `goBack()`).

  **3. The conflict check is skipped after a generation that did not finish, and
  another edit gets overwritten.**
  - Where: ContentView.vue:143–152.
  - What's wrong: when the stream ends with an `error` or a Stop, the queued
  step still returns `campaigns.get(id)`. The save queue adopts whatever version
  comes back, but `fromCampaign` is not called, so the editors keep the old
  text.
  - Scenario: the same draft is edited in another tab while the AI is writing.
  The server's save fails as stale and sends `error`. The page silently takes
  the newer version, the editors stay stale (empty in my test), and the next
  blur overwrites the other tab's edit. No reload banner appears.
  - Same root cause: if Stop arrives just after the server saved, the page says
  "Stopped. Nothing was saved", but Review then shows the new AI text.
  - Fix: inside the queued step, if `!finished` and the refetched version
  differs from the version sent, throw `new ApiError(..., 409)`. That puts the
  page in the existing conflict state with its Reload banner. Otherwise call
  `fromCampaign` whenever the saved content changed.
  - Verified (r2 A: "OTHER TAB EDIT" was replaced by "MY LATER EDIT", no warning
  banner shown).

  #### Minor (Nice to Have)

  **4. A 409 on Review shows a generic message with no Reload button.**
  - Where: ReviewView.vue:97–98 and 125.
  - What's wrong: the marketer sees "The server didn't accept this change.
  Details (in English): the campaign changed…". The Content step shows its own
  text and a Reload button for the same case.
  - Fix: when the error is a 409, show `t('error.changed')` and a Reload button,
  like ContentView does.
  - Verified (r2 B/C, in English and Turkish).

  **5. The schedule checks don't re-run as time passes.**
  - Where: ReviewView.vue:69.
  - What's wrong: `scheduleProblem(at, new Date())` inside a computed only
  re-runs when some other value changes.
  - Scenario: the marketer picks a time 2 minutes ahead (or the brief's
  suggested time is close) and waits. The page still allows approval, and the
  server rejects it with an English message.
  - This is safe, because the server checks again. Fix: re-check `Date.now()`
  when the button is clicked, or use a ticking `now` ref.
  - Traced.

  **6. The confirm dialog shows the time without its time zone.**
  - Where: ReviewView.vue:214, using formatDateTime.
  - What's wrong: the zone appears only on the field label.
  - Also: a time that doesn't exist in a spring-forward DST hour is quietly
  shifted by `new Date(local)`. The dialog does show the shifted time.
  - Fix: add `timeZoneName: 'short'`, or repeat `browserZone()` in the dialog.
  - Traced.

  **7. Accessibility details.**
  - Where: ReviewView.vue:195.
  - What's wrong: the blockers list is `role="alert"` and also receives focus,
  so it is announced twice. It is announced again every time the list changes,
  for example when the review box is ticked. Fix: drop `role="alert"` and keep
  the focus move.
  - Also: in ContentView.vue:213 the streaming `role="status"` element is
  created at the same moment it gets its text, which some screen readers don't
  announce. Fix: keep one status region mounted all the time.
  - Plausible.

  **8. The skip estimate on Content can be out of date.**
  - Where: ContentView.vue:245 and 277.
  - What's wrong: the estimate is based on the *saved* content. Until the next
  save it doesn't reflect what the marketer is typing.
  - Effect: low, since it is labelled "Estimate".
  - Traced.

  **9. Test gaps.**
  - What's covered: the pure helpers and the stream client have tests.
  - What's missing: the view logic. That is `write()` with the save queue (done,
  error, abort, refetch), commit's mapping of field errors, and
  `openConfirm`/`approve`. Today only the Playwright script outside the repo
  covers it.
  - Fix: one component test per view, mounted with stubbed
  `campaigns`/`streamContent`, would pin issues 1–3 and the approval guards.

  ### Declined to judge
  - Two fictional preview samples instead of the design's three
  (shortest/typical/longest): this was the engineer's decision and the README
  records it.
  - Review and the confirm dialog split into page plus one dialog instead of the
  design's layout: recorded in the README.
  - English server details shown inside translated errors: this is policy D44.
  - The page falling back to an error when the estimate request fails on Review:
  an edge case the spec doesn't cover, with low impact.
  - Review being reachable when the content is incomplete: the blockers already
  stop approval.
  - No ordering guard if a `done` event is followed by a concurrent edit before
  the refetch: the window is milliseconds, below what matters.
  - Brief text, AI content and the schedule note are shown exactly as written,
  with no translation: as required.
  - Pixel-level match to boards 05/06: I only checked the structure and wording,
  which match.
  - Go and backend behaviour: not changed in this range.

  ### Recommendations
  - Fix issues 1–3 with a small change in ContentView. Together they protect
  against losing the marketer's text, which is the main risk in this step.
  - Add one component test per view.
  - Fix 4 and 5 together: they are the main UX gaps on Review, and both are safe
  because the server checks again.

  ### Assessment — Ready to merge? With fixes
  Sending without explicit approval is not possible, and the version check makes
  sure the marketer approves what they reviewed. But the Content step can lose
  or overwrite the marketer's text: issues 1 and 2 verified by direct test,
  issue 3 shown with a second tab editing the same draft. These should be fixed
  before merge.

  I did not check pixel-level design fidelity or screen-reader output in real
  assistive technology.

  version shown on Review, so a change made elsewhere is rejected rather than
  approved. Your instruction for T17 didn't say to fix findings, so I haven't

  Important: all three are on the Content step and can lose the marketer's text

  1. "Write with AI" overwrites unsaved typing without asking. The confirmation
     only appears when saved content exists. If a marketer types the SMS while
     the email is still empty (so nothing has autosaved yet), their text is
     replaced silently. Verified in a browser.
  2. Edits can be lost when leaving without a click. Saving happens when a field
     loses focus. Clicking a link saves first (verified), but the browser's

       checking whether it changed. If the same draft was edited in another tab

  ## Re-review of T17 fixes (58f0bfb..185839b)

  **What I ran:** `web-test` passed (22 files, 258 tests, type check clean).
  `make e2e` passed (76 PASS, "ALL BROWSER CHECKS PASSED"). I also ran my own
  Playwright scripts (scratchpad
  `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-[user]-smart-campaign-composer/b26c288a-6558-4a4
  0-b2cd-ebdb9a1b6414/scratchpad/re-review-t17/`). One of my scripts crashed
  partway and left four drafts (249–252); I deleted them with a filtered
  cleanup. All test drafts I created (234, 249–256) are deleted.

  ### Fix verification
  1. **AI overwrite confirm: Fixed.** `needsOverwriteConfirm` asks when saved
  content exists or any chosen field has text. The e2e checks the unsaved-typing
  case and that Cancel keeps the text.
  2. **Lost edits on leaving: Fixed for normal leaving.** Verified:
     - Typing, then clicking the Review step link right away: saved (`startXY`).
     - Typing, then `goBack()`: saved (e2e).
     - Router push to another campaign's content page: campaign A saved, B
  untouched, the page shows B.
     - Incomplete text, then Back: confirm appears and "stay" keeps the text.
     - Tab close: `beforeunload` prompt appears.
     - Leaving while the AI writes: localized confirm; dismiss keeps streaming,
  accept stops it and saves nothing (version stayed 2).

     `onBeforeRouteUpdate` is correct: `AppLayout` keys `RouterView` by path,
  and the guard fires on the old instance. This fix introduced the Critical bug
  below.
  3. **Unfinished generation adopting a newer version: Fixed.** The e2e (PUT
  from "elsewhere" during streaming) shows the conflict banner, keeps the other
  edit and leaves the fields read-only. Reload clears the saver's conflict
  state.
  4. **Review 409: Fixed.** Both approve 409s (`approve.go:133,136`) mean
  "changed or approved elsewhere", so the warning plus Reload fits. The e2e
  covers it.
  5. **Schedule checks re-run over time: Fixed.** A 15 s tick, cleared on
  unmount, and `now` is refreshed in `openConfirm` and `approve`.
  6. **Time zone in the confirm dialog: Fixed.** `timeZoneName: 'short'`;
  covered by a unit test and the e2e.
  7. **A11y: Partly fixed.**
     - The blockers list and the single persistent `role=status` are done.
     - `ContentView.vue` still mounts `<p v-if="incomplete" role="status">`
  together with its text, the pattern the review flagged.
     - `statusText` gives `aiProblem` priority until the next AI run, so after
  an AI error "Messages saved." is never announced.
     - `notice` stays `'content.saved'`, so the second and later saves are not
  re-announced.
  9. **Test gap: Fixed.** The e2e suite exists, runs and is mostly honest;
  caveats below.

  ### New issues

  **Critical: an expired session during content editing causes an endless save
  loop and endless confirm dialogs (verified).**
  - Where: `ContentView.vue` `beforeLeaving`/`commit`, together with `api.ts:47`
  `onUnauthorized` and `router.ts` `setUnauthorizedHandler`.
  - Scenario: the session expires and the marketer edits. The debounced save
  gets a 401, the handler pushes to /login, and the leave guard runs `commit()`.
  That save gets a 401 too, which pushes again and calls the guard again,
  without end. Each round also calls `window.confirm`, because the failed save
  makes `commit()` return false.
  - Measured: cookies cleared, one `fill`, then 8 s: **650 PUTs, 648 confirm
  dialogs**, and the page never reached /login. In a real browser the marketer
  gets a blocking confirm over and over, and neither OK nor Cancel ends it.
  - Fix: don't save or ask when leaving for a public route, e.g.
  `onBeforeRouteLeave((to) => (to.meta.public ? true : beforeLeaving()))`.
  Better still, give `commit()` a "can't save" result on 401 so it never queues
  another save. Add an e2e that clears cookies and edits.

  **Important: a queued save sends text read at run time without re-checking it
  (verified).**
  - Where: `ContentView.vue` `commit`. `complete()` and `unsaved()` are checked
  when `commit` is called, but `chosen()` is read when the queued op runs.
  - Scenario: a save is in flight (slow PUT), a second save is queued, then the
  marketer clears the SMS. The queued save sends `""` and gets a 422: "The
  server didn't accept this change… must not be empty". Without this race the
  page would show the friendly incomplete note.
  - Fix: re-check `complete()` and `unsaved()` inside the `saver.save` op and
  return the current campaign when there is nothing valid to save.
  - Positive: rapid typing during an in-flight save never sends stale text. The
  last PUT carried the latest text, with no 409s.

  **Minor**
  - **Redundant saves (verified).** The debounce timer isn't cleared by blur or
  other commits, and every call that sees "unsaved" while a save is in flight
  queues another one. One edit session produced 4 PUTs, 3 of them identical
  (`"one two three"`). Each PUT bumps the version, refreshes the estimate and
  widens the window for 409s from other tabs. Fix: clear the timer in
  `commit()`, and/or do the re-check inside the op described above.
  - **False "changed elsewhere" (plausible).** If Stop aborts after the server
  saved the AI's text but before the `done` event arrives, `generationOutcome`
  returns `'conflict'` and the marketer's own AI result is reported as someone
  else's change. The code comment admits this. It is acceptable because Reload
  shows the right data, but the wording is wrong for that case.
  - **Silent block in `approve()` (ReviewView).** If `now` makes the chosen time
  stale while the confirm dialog is open, `approve()` returns with no focus
  move or announcement. The list is no longer `role=alert`, so a screen-reader
  user gets no feedback. Fix: focus `blockerList` as `openConfirm` does.
  - **e2e honesty and reliability.**
    - `check(true, …)` after a `waitFor` is tautological; it still fails via the
  thrown timeout, but reads as a fake check.
    - The Stop and conflict-during-generation tests rely on the mock's 30 ms
  chunk pacing (`cmd/scc/main.go:93`). If streaming finishes first, Stop is
  never clicked and the suite stops. This is a mild flake risk.
    - `waitForTimeout(400/500/800)` follow-up sleeps are used in 8 places.
    - The content step's route-update guard (another campaign's content page)
  and leaving while the AI writes are not covered by e2e. I checked both
  manually and they work.
  - **e2e side effects on a developer's database.**
    - It approves a real scheduled campaign (tomorrow 10:00) and relies on
  `finally` to cancel it. Ctrl-C or a killed process skips cleanup and leaves it
  scheduled; it would go through whatever provider is configured.
    - The cancelled test campaigns accumulate in the list; the README documents
  this.
    - The README doesn't say the suite assumes the mock AI. With a real
  Anthropic key it would make paid calls and its brief-dependent checks would
  likely fail. Add a line saying it needs mock mode, or make the suite check
  `/api/me`'s AI mode and stop.
  - **Docs.** The README paragraphs on the content step and on Test match the
  behaviour I saw, except for the mock-AI assumption above.

  ### Declined to judge
  - Real screen-reader output for `aria-labelledby` on a focused `ul`.
  - Behaviour with the real Anthropic provider.
  - The mobile Back gesture on real devices; I tested `history.back` and
  `goBack` only.

  ### Explicit approval guarantee
  Still intact. `approve()` only runs from the confirm dialog's `@confirm`; the
  blockers are re-checked with a fresh `now`; "Go back" approves nothing and a
  stale version approves nothing (both verified in the e2e). Nothing
  auto-approves.

  redundant saves are small and worth doing in the same change.

  Fixed after the re-review (browser tests written first and seen to fail)

  - Expired session: leaving without a session no longer saves or asks, and a
    401 ends a save quietly. Now: 2 save attempts, 0 dialogs, and the marketer
    reaches sign-in. Before the fix the same test measured 297 saves and 295
    dialogs.
  - Stale queued save: a queued save rechecks its content when it runs, so text
    cleared meanwhile is never sent. Before the fix, 2 empty saves were
    rejected.
  - Redundant saves: one edit is one save now; before, 3 of 4 requests were
    identical.
  - Review: if the chosen time goes stale while the confirmation is open, focus
    moves to what's missing instead of failing silently.
  - Browser suite: it refuses to run unless the app is in mock mode, so it can't
    spend real AI credits. The README says so.
